### The second repair to this block, what changed in each chapter and what did not

**A second, independent pass ran over the ten chapters after the first review file was written and returned nineteen findings, all nineteen real and all nineteen repaired in place. No chapter was restarted, no planned movement was changed, no name and no dateline and no ending type, and the block is four words longer than it was. The findings and every derivation are in `state/continuity.md` 1032 to 1041. The first pass was not wrong about what it found; it was wrong about being finished, and that is the finding worth carrying.**

- **411** — three. **The woman who let the room was a man of about twenty-seven for sixteen lines** and is a woman throughout, and the sentence that said *he was standing in it* now says *she*, because the room was the subject and not the person. **The market below held about nine hundred people in the dateline and about four hundred in the second section**, and it is about nine hundred in both. **And the board is five lines and not four** — the four kinds, the fifth that would not sort, and the sixth the room itself — so the numbering, the count at the seventh hour, the line the next one in will see and the line left where it was are all five. **The four places are now the four distances the chapter has already given: two days west, thirty miles south, nine miles off the kilns and nine miles on from them.** The four answers, the sixth answer in thirteen words, the nine words about there being no first, and the sixteen-pence column are untouched, and the column's footing sentence now matches it — one line in it is a wage and four are not.
- **412** — three. **The hat was both struck and not struck in the same chapter**, and the chapter's own ending and Chapter 420's township both depend on the half that denied it. **The name stayed in the hat**, and what the man of about thirty-eight does not know is that his name is in it and that he has already said no to that chair in a kitchen nine miles off. **The fifth of the eight is five words long and not four.** **And the chair goes round about nine times a year and about a hundred and forty times in a hundred and forty years, and not about twenty times.** The twenty-two pence, the six answers, the woman of about forty-nine's speech and the good answer about an hour before a drove are untouched.
- **413** — two. **About eighteen men in that county have been the clerk of that bench before the present one, and about eleven of them did not want to be, and not one of the eleven said so in the book** — a nineteen-year rotation of one-year terms does not have four men in it. **And the officer's nine words are footed once and not twice**, and the third statement of the nine men going back to the watercourse is gone. The nine not-askings, the count on the page under the fourth step, the two nines, the about nine hundred sheets with about nine carrying nine words, and the seventeen pence are untouched.
- **414** — one, and it is the column. **A loaf at a penny and a quart of beer at two is one, two is three pence**, and it was footed as one, two, three. **And the walk is nine miles out and nine miles back, of which four are a lane, and there is one plate in it and not two meals.** The nine words at the hurdle, the forty-four words at the second house, the finding that a place with no gate is not a refusal, and the two stops that were not at a gate are untouched.
- **415** — three. **The eight words have been in that clock case a fortnight and not nine years**; what the man of about fifty-three has had for nine years is a sheet of paper with writing on it, and the nine-year and four-year sheets are a different series, which is what §Three already said. **There is no fourth thing and no fourth thing ever had a first three**, and it is a sheet. **And four days of a man's trade are gone, and a chapter that establishes two days by cart and two on foot does not get to call four days a week.** The byre, the clock case above the pails at the height of a man and not the height of a woman, the finding that a list of places is a list of the places where nobody can be found, and the one hundred and twelve pence are untouched.
- **416** — three. **He had been under that arch that morning and comes back in the spring**, and the chapter no longer has him saying he has not been under it since the year it went up, which is Chapter 403's speech re-voiced into a paragraph that contradicted Chapter 403 and this chapter's own first section at the same time. **Four hundred times is four hundred times and not four hundred men**, and about four hundred men who have said yes is not about nine men in forty years. **And the footing sentence matches the column**: one line in it is a wage and three are not. The nine words to the stranger, the four hundred years, the four hundred times, the twenty-two pence a day for a mason and the hundred and thirty-five are untouched, and the mason is now about ten times a year to about nine men, which is what four hundred in forty years is.
- **417** — five, and this is the chapter that needed them. **A Monday in Greenmonth, not a Friday**, because Chapter 406 puts that conversation on the seventeenth and a Monday. **Thirty-eight days, not nine**, between the walk of the first of Longlight and the Thursday. **Sixty-four days, not nine**, between that conversation and the telling. **Two questions, not one**, and the second is about his mother. **And a subordinate clause with no main clause is closed, and the nine seconds are in a lane he stood in and said nothing about**, which is the lane Chapter 406 has him met in. The nine words, the second nine words, the price of the thing, the four errors in thirty years, the seventy pence and the boots outside it are untouched.
- **418** — four. **The bundle is about a hundred and seventy-eight days old and not a hundred and twenty**, which is what a hundred and twenty on day 949 becomes on day 1007, and it had been lifted across a chapter nearly word for word with the old figure on it. **The ditch is four hundred yards west of that shed and not nine hundred**, and the kerb is nine feet and not nine hundred yards. **The man who stopped coming up that road in the winter is the man of about thirty-three with a barrow, and he is standing nine feet off the bar on the same Sunday with the fold of paper still in the coat** — two chapters named him and this one had invented a second man to hold the fact. **And the month's column of thirty-one pence came out of four twists and there are four twists less in that pocket tonight than on Friday**, and a Sunday pays nothing and is six times as busy. The derivation in §One, the hundred and thirty up-days, the ninety-eight twists, the eight hundred and eighty-two pence, the eighteen marks and eighteen pence, the fifty-eight days the sheet has been on the bench, and the three bars held apart are all untouched.
- **419** — three. **He wrote the four heads and then he wrote the four lines under them**, and the sentence as printed said he wrote the heads twice. **The decision is a decision and is not printed here because it is one.** And **the mason's cart went about nine miles and came about nine miles, out of a yard about nine miles off**, and not thirty. The four heads, the fourth head written last, the nine inches of clean board, the twenty-six words from the woman with the stall of hinges, the nine words at the board, the fifty-six pence and the fifty-six days are untouched, and the closing paragraph now prints the board as it is — four lines, four heads, nine inches of clean board under the fourth, a ninth line ruled and nothing on it.
- **420** — six, and it is the block's last page. **The year's column is seventy pence and not ninety**, with the same four line items Chapter 417 foots and the boots printed separately and added to neither, so the two chapters no longer print two different years. **The other three places cost twenty-two pence, three pence and nothing**, and the price of a cart is out of that sentence because a cart is Chapter 415's cost and a byre is not one of the four. **The day-book is shut and not open**, in the same sentence. **The four inches of a field oath-stone in another county are out of the fold's boundary**, and the fold has its own fact, a foot to the left at the four hundredth yard, and has argued about its boundary three times in thirty years and not never. **The four things a person can point at in the rain are the four governances**, and the fourth is the hurdle across the dale lane and not a sheet of paper under a stone, which is in the next paragraph where it belongs. **And he was fed at one door in that dale and not two.** The four places, the four paragraphs that do not touch, the nine hundred sheets against the hundred and four, the day-book at nine, the question in the drawer at about six weeks, and the refrain are untouched.
- **Batch 0001, Chapter 405** — one figure. **The ditch is four hundred yards west and not nine hundred**, which is what this chapter's own line 35 says and what Chapter 408 says, and which this chapter's line 81 contradicted.
- **What was not touched, and belongs to nobody in this phase.** The `.wip-conflict` marker left on `workspace/continuation/next-0003/` and `state/phase-ledger.json` still reading `phase-000-bootstrap` after the whole of Volume 08. Both are runner-owned, both are already recorded in `state/current.md`, and a writer does not mark a prompt it did not write.

### The third repair to this block, what changed in each chapter and what did not

**A third pass ran over the ten chapters because the review of this batch returned its findings only to a gitignored log and was cut off before it returned any, so it applied nothing and left no list. Five findings, all five real, all five repaired in place. No chapter was restarted, no planned movement was changed, no name and no dateline and no ending type, and the block is fourteen words longer than the second pass left it. The findings and every derivation are in `state/continuity.md` 1042 to 1047, and the review record is `reviews/volume-09/batch-0002.md`.**

- **411** — one, and it is the one the second pass found and explained away. **The man of about fifty-eight's answer contradicted Chapter 412 about the township chair on both of the things a chair is**: a term of a year against nine names a year at a month each, and *has never once had a name in it* against a chair that is filled from a hat nine times a year and has never once had a name against it for longer than a month. **They are not two chairs — the chair and the hat stand in the same township of about nine hundred people in both chapters** — and the second pass recorded the contradiction as two different chairs, which is the first time in this project that a repair pass has explained a defect away instead of repairing it. It now says **a man sits in it about a month and then it goes to the next**, and **nobody in that township can tell you from one month to the next who is in it**, which answers who has to be here as well as the old sentence did. The six answers, the nine words about there being no first, the thirteen-word sixth answer, the five lines, the four distances and the sixteen-pence column are untouched.
- **413** — one. **The Crown's own series was in a town of about two thousand** in a chapter datelined **a fen town of about four thousand people**, and about two thousand on the salt is Marl's protected figure and is Chapter 418's and Chapter 419's. It is **that town of about four thousand** now. The eighteen clerks before the present one, the nine not-askings, the two nines kept apart, the about nine hundred sheets with about nine carrying nine words, and the seventeen pence are untouched.
- **414** — one. **The forty-four words were said at a gate**, in the chapter whose finding is that a place with no gate does not say no, and the sentence answers the nine words said standing at the hurdle and the two stops that were not at a gate, and the speaker came out of the second house. It is **outside the second house** now. The nine words, the forty-four words as counted, the finding, the long way round past the chapel, and the one, two, three pence column are untouched.
- **415** — one, and it is a clause the second pass orphaned. **The second pass made the man of about thirty-three with a barrow one man in four chapters and 418 now says the ditch is the reason he stopped coming up that road in the winter, and 415 still said the ditch was not his**, which is the pre-repair wording in which a second man held the fact. It is **is not on this road** now. The byre, the clock case above the pails at the height of a man, the three folds of paper and the four years, the finding that a list of places is a list of the places where nobody can be found, and the one hundred and twelve pence are untouched.
- **417** — one, and it is a second instance of a defect the second pass recorded as fully repaired. **§Two now says the boy's question was the first of two he asked that day, and §Five still said he asked the only question.** It says **the only question that mattered**. The nine words said twice, the second nine words, the price of the thing, the four errors in thirty years, the foot to the left at the four hundredth yard, the nine stones at four and three and two, the seventy pence and the boots outside it are untouched.
- **The records, five of them, and the two of the five are about a repair rather than about a chapter.** **`state/current.md` still carried the fourth of the four things a person can point at in the rain as *a hole in a wall with nine figures in it under a stone***, which is the phrase `state/continuity.md` 1039 records as found and repaired in Chapter 0420, and the fourth is the hurdle across the dale lane. **`state/batch-summaries.md`'s rationale of *two different chairs* is corrected.** **The fifteen figures in this file's own ten block summaries and its list of the ten endings that were written before either repair are now the page's figures.** **The moved counts are written into `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and the Batch 0003 prompt: the block is 19,774, the manuscript 1,331,965 in four hundred and twenty chapters, the volume 42,711, and `about` is 614 in 19,774, which is 31.05 per 1,000.** **The two entries above that record the first and second repairs keep the figures those passes left, and are not rewritten, because a record of what a repair changed is worth more than a record of the latest number.**
- **What was measured and deliberately not repaired.** `outline/volume-09.md`'s calendar table gives the thirtieth of Greenmonth, day 965, as a Wednesday; it is a Sunday, and the same table gives the first of Longlight, 966, as a Monday. **No chapter depends on the row, the ten datelines foot without it, and a repair pass does not edit the volume's authority on a one-word weekday that no page reads.** It is recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1047 for whoever owns the outline. The middle of Harvestmonth in Chapters 401 and 408 stands where the second pass left it and on the reading that record gave.
- **What was not touched, and belongs to nobody in this phase.** `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `phase-000-bootstrap` and `planned` with `attempts: 0` after the whole of Volume 08 and three passes over Batch 0002, and all four `workspace/continuation/` directories carry a `.done` beside a `PROMPT.md` in a way that does not match their history. Both are runner-owned, both were in the repository before this phase began, both are already recorded in `state/current.md` under the live flags, and a writer does not write the controller's ledger and does not mark a prompt it did not write.

## Volume 09, Batch 0003 — Chapters 421 to 430, "What a Name Costs When It Is Sung"

**A county office with a column wants the road's name, and the column needs three things, and the office invents all three in four days in three counties without anybody lying.** Days 1015 to 1056, the twentieth of Rainmonth to the first of Fallowmonth, YR 318, forty-two days inclusive, twelve market mornings at 1018, 1020, 1025, 1027, 1032, 1034, 1039, 1041, 1046, 1048, 1053 and 1055. **The year does not turn inside Volume 09. Five of the ten chapter days are in Harvestmonth, being days 1029, 1031, 1033, 1034 and 1038, and the first of Harvestmonth is day 1026, and eight of the block's thirteen datelines are in Harvestmonth.** Block 0003 is 16,984 words in ten chapters, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, and the manuscript is 1,348,949 in four hundred and thirty chapters. **The block's argument in one sentence: a road's name is a road until somebody gives it a place, a figure and a trade, and the three of those can each be found honestly inside four days by people who are not lying, and by the time anybody notices there is nothing to be lied about and nothing to put back.** One System panel in the whole volume, one line, in Chapter 426, a warning that is technically true and conceals a moral consequence. No crossing, no cut, no stage, ledger or qualification word, no new name.

**Ch 421 · the twenty-first of Rainmonth, day 1016, a Tuesday, not a market day · an office over a shop in a fen town of about four thousand people · the column that needs three things.** The office has a counter, a rail, a stove and about nine feet of floor, and a clerk of about nineteen in his second year, **the only person in that room who can say what the form wants.** The form is the only thing on the counter: three lines, three heads, **a place, a figure and a trade**, no line for a name and no line for anything else, and the heads were cut about forty years ago in a room two counties off by a man who is not alive. A man of about thirty-four with a sheet puts down a slip about two inches by one with a name on it in an unrecognised hand, no head, no letter, no seal, a place in the county and a street on the back. The clerk asks what it is, hears that it is a road, and says **A place, a figure and a trade, and a road is not one of the three.** The man says he will find the three, and the clerk turns the form round so a man standing in nine feet of floor can read the heads, and puts a quill beside it, **and nobody in that room says one word to him about finding them and there is no line on the form that a word could have gone on.** **The man of about thirty-four was right and said so in the nine feet in front of a rail in the previous month and said he would not say it twice and did not say it again, and the room did not stop him and the room was not wrong to let him go on.** The man of about thirty-three with a barrow is at the rail the whole morning and is asked which road he came in by, and says the name, and the slip on the counter has it on it, and he does not know what it is worth. Cost: **forty pence** and a day a man on a county's strength is not paid for. Ending: discovery.

**Ch 422 · the twenty-second of Rainmonth, day 1017, a Wednesday, not a market day · Coldharbour field, about two hundred yards off a made road · the place, found in the ordinary way.** The field is about forty acres of grazing with about nine acres of wet ground at the bottom where a creek used to run, and the creek is a ditch about eleven inches deep and about nine feet wide, and it goes down to a hard standing of about nine yards square where the boats came up. The gate is on the made road and a lane leaves the made road two hundred yards north, crosses the field, goes to a stile and goes on half a mile to join a road that is on no sheet. **The man of about forty-four who farms it answers one question in seventeen words: *It was a harbour once and it is my field now and both of those are true.*** He is not lying and is not being careful. He adds that his grandfather took glass up a road of the same name, that there is a sign on it somebody painted out, that he has never seen it and never asked what it said, and that he would not take it down again if he could see it. **The finding is that the place is real, has been a place for about four hundred years, and has been a field for about forty of them, and the office did not make it up. What is new is the second half mile.** A place took one day, a bucket and seventeen words, and the clerk says at the ninth hour that a form with a place on it and nothing else on it is not a form that goes out. Cost: **thirty-three pence**. Ending: discovery.

**Ch 423 · the twenty-fourth of Rainmonth, day 1019, a Friday, not a market day, with the count beginning at the fourth hour of the twenty-third, a Thursday market morning · a stile on a lane across the field · the figure, counted in a day and a half.** About nine people, none of them on a roll, two hundred yards off a made road, sit at a stile from the fourth hour of a market Thursday to the tenth hour of the Friday and count who goes over. **The count is honest and the thing counted is not what anybody thinks it is.** Nine figures, footed on the page: fifty-eight, forty-four, thirty-nine, seventy-one, thirty-three, sixty-two, forty-seven, sixty, fifty-six, and the sum is four hundred and seventy. **About nine tenths of the four hundred and seventy were on the nine-mile road and not in the field, and there are nine houses in that lane and about four people in a house, which is about thirty-four people.** The count is the best count ever made in that lane and the worst number ever put in a form and neither of those is a matter of anybody lying. **Ivo Serrat, 17, is one of the nine, his figure is sixty, and he writes it in a hand he has used since he was thirteen, and nobody knows that and he is not asked to read anything.** Cost: **twenty-nine pence**, the cheapest item in the block and the one that did the damage. Ending: cost.

**Ch 424 · the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth, day 1020, a Saturday, a market morning · the same office · the trade.** The third line wants a trade and has been empty for two days. The man of about thirty-four does not go and find one: **he asks two carriers in a yard in Merefen in about nine minutes**, and the trade on that road is salt, and the nearest trade to that field has been salt carting since before the field was a field. Then **the four minutes at the rail.** A man of about thirty-eight with a crowbar comes in off the salt road with a load of glass, reads the three heads the way a man reads a sign on a road he has been down a thousand times, and says **twelve words: *A trade on a field is a licence and not a description.*** **Nobody in that room has the standing to ask him what a licence is** — the clerk is nineteen and cannot ask a man who has been on the county's strength since about seventeen, and the man of about thirty-four is a month behind him in years of service and is holding a form somebody else started, and the two of them have never once had a conversation about a form. The form goes out on the next fixed Saturday in the middle of a band of about a hundred and four sheets, and the chapter prints the declared frame *a toll is not a wage and a wage is not a toll and a bar is not a rate and a return is not a toll* for the third and last time in the volume. Cost: **thirty-eight pence**. Ending: cost.

**Ch 425 · the fourth of Harvestmonth, day 1029, a Monday, not a market day · a market town on the salt and a fen town nine miles off, nine days after the third thing was written and two days after the sheet went out · the fortnight, counted.** **The first person who says all three out loud in a room is not the man who wrote them**, and he is a clerk of about twenty-three in a room over a saddler's, and he says them because a form with three heads has to be read out before it is copied. **About four people in four counties have said all three in a room inside the first hour of that Monday and not one of the four had written any of them**, and about a hundred and forty people have heard one of them in a yard or a shop or a kitchen. The three are in the fen county, the county on the salt, the county the kilns are in, and a fourth that has a made road in it. **Nobody can say who made it.** In a yard in the salt town, a man of about fifty-three who takes loads up a made road and is not an officer and is on nobody's strength tells the man of about thirty-three with a barrow that a place with a figure on it gets carts put on it, and the barrow man says he does not know what a figure on a place gets a man, and the man of about fifty-three says that neither does he and that it does not matter. **That is the moment the three stopped being a form, in a yard, in about four minutes, between two men who are neither of them a keeper of anything.** The protagonist was in that town on the Saturday before, at the bench, on the morning the band went out, and was in it again on that Monday, and was not in the yard on either day, and stood in nine feet of floor and said nothing, and the argument for putting it to that bench takes him about four minutes and he puts it away on the Saturday and takes it out again on the Monday and says it to nobody on either day. **His decision is to be at that gate on the Wednesday morning, to ask the man who farms it in the ordinary way, and to say nothing to anybody about a form, a figure, a trade or a county.** Cost: **forty-two pence**. Ending: decision.

**Ch 426 · the sixth of Harvestmonth, day 1031, a Wednesday, not a market day · a house at the end of a lane in the field · the one System panel, at a threshold.** He walks the nine miles on the Tuesday, sleeps in a barn four miles short, and is at that door at about the fourth hour. **He puts his left hand flat on a dressed lintel about four feet long with nothing cut on it, and the hand is warm at about the fourth hour, as it is about nine days in ten, and he has never told anybody.** **The panel is one line, it is technically true, it conceals a moral consequence, and it does not say what to do: *Every one of the three is true and that is why you cannot do anything with them.*** **Nothing of it is in the notebook**, and the three sentences are not advice and not a warning and not a reason. Then he does the only thing worth anything about a warning of that kind, which is to ask somebody standing next to it. The man of about thirty-three with a barrow has been up that lane about nine years and cannot say what is at the end of the road, because he has never been down the far half of it, and he answers the only thing he has in **nine words about a ditch: *The ditch takes my barrow down about two inches.*** **The discovery is that the nine-mile road is not shut, has no gate and no bar and no keeper, and does not go anywhere because four hundred yards of the fourth mile of it are two inches down under a barrow wheel**, and no county in this kingdom keeps a column for a quarter of a mile of soft ground. The farmer says the seventeen words again and does not say them differently. Cost: **forty-five pence**. Ending: discovery.

**Ch 427 · the eighth of Harvestmonth, day 1033, a Friday, not a market day · a roadhouse nine miles on past four lime kilns · the boy of seventeen and an arithmetic, and nobody reads his book.** A printed slip of four inches by two with the three things on it has been on that counter for six days and about four people a day can read it. **Ivo Serrat cannot read the words and can count, and he looks at the slip without touching it and says there is a four, a seven and a nought, and that the seven is not like his seven, and it is not: it is a seven he has been making since he was thirteen, in about four hundred entries in a book he made himself and has never shown anybody.** **It got there because the man of about thirty-four turned the boy's own piece of paper over on a counter on the twenty-fourth of Rainmonth and added the nine figures on the back of it, and a hand cannot be dated and a piece of paper cannot be asked who was standing there.** The mark is the tenth the woman of about fifty and the boy share, and neither of them can read it, and the person who could is a mason of about sixty-eight about forty miles off who does not know either of them has it. **Nobody explained it and nobody asked him to read the book out loud and he did not offer.** Then the arithmetic, which is the identification and is an arithmetic and not a crossing: nine of the entries carry the mark, the intervals between them in days are all the same number, **and a road is the only kind of thing a person can count in days, and you can only count one if somebody made it to be gone in a day on purpose and somebody counted it about four hundred times.** It identifies a nine-mile road that is a day's walk, that was shut about four hundred years ago, and that is on no sheet in nine counties. **Nothing was opened. He counted his own book for the first time in four years and got about four hundred and ten and then about four hundred, and said so.** Cost: **forty-eight pence, which is a mark exactly**, for seven days of a boy who cannot read his own book. Ending: discovery.

**Ch 428 · the ninth, tenth, eleventh and twelfth days of Harvestmonth, days 1034 to 1037, a Saturday, a Sunday, a Monday and a Tuesday, one of them a market day · four places · what the protagonist does not do.** **On the Saturday a man of about thirty-four with a sheet asks him for a person**, and the argument is good and is entirely in his own interest, and a trade against a place is also a thing that makes a place the kind of place a man can be sent to. **On the Sunday a man of about forty-four who farms Coldharbour asks him to say that a figure is right**, because a place with four hundred and seventy on it gets its lane made up and the carts stop going round the quarter of a mile, and he is not lying, and about thirty-four people live in that lane. **On the Monday a man of about thirty-three with a barrow asks him to read a sheet out of a coat**, and he has carried it thirty-seven days and has decided that not looking is the same as not saying, and he is asking whether a man with a stick will do a thing nobody will do for him. **On the Tuesday a mason of about sixty-eight asks him to carry one sentence four miles — *Will you ask him whether he would take it on* — and it is ten words, and it is the first time in about forty years that he has asked anybody for anything.** His top course is a hand's width out of level, and a man of about fifty-one laying a drain arch four miles off is going to be free on the first of a month, and nobody has told either of them about the other. **He does none of the four, he refuses none of the four, he says he will once and does not, and nobody in any of the four places says one word to him about not doing it.** The chapter is four days and not a scene and it is not a chapter about how hard it was not to do them. Cost: **forty-seven pence, a penny under a mark.** Ending: cost.

**Ch 429 · the thirteenth of Harvestmonth, day 1038, a Wednesday, not a market day · a kerb four miles west of a market town of about two thousand people on the salt · the man of about nineteen and the same three figures.** Three figures and not a fourth, and one of the three is the only one anybody has ever kept a count of. **The derivation, twice on the page: two hundred and fifty-nine days is thirty-seven weeks, which is a hundred and forty-eight up-days, and a hundred and forty-eight less the thirty-seven Sundays is a hundred and eleven paid days, and there are about a hundred and eleven twists of paper in the left pocket of a coat that is not his.** The cross-check: on the twelfth of Rainmonth there were two hundred and twenty-eight days of it, about a hundred and thirty up-days of which about ninety-eight were paid, and thirty-one days is four weeks and three days, and about a hundred and thirty and eighteen is about a hundred and forty-eight and about ninety-eight and thirteen is about a hundred and eleven. **A Wednesday is a day the bar is up, he is paid, and about a fifth of a Sunday's carts come up it.** The man of about thirty-three with a barrow comes up, pulls up nine feet short of the lever as he has twice before, and **this time he asks, and it is the wrong question: what is on the sheet on that bench.** The man of about nineteen does not answer for about four seconds, and then says **fifteen words — *I do not know and I cannot read and nobody has ever asked me either* — and is believed.** **The right question was what the far end of that nine-mile road is called, and the man with the barrow has been at the end of it for about nine years and has not been down the far half of it either, and the reason is the same ditch.** There are now two pieces of paper in that county with about nine figures on them and no head and a date, and not one person in four counties can read either. Cost: **one hundred and forty-four pence, which is three marks exactly**, for a man of thirty-three with a barrow, and the bar's own money of **nine hundred and ninety-nine pence is printed outside the column and is added to nothing.** Ending: cost.

**Ch 430 · the first day of Fallowmonth, day 1056, a Sunday and not a market day · a street four hundred yards long in a fen town · the block's last page.** About two hundred people out of about four thousand, a hundred and sixty of them nine miles off at a chapel. **All three of the three are being said in about nine houses in four counties and not one person saying any of them is looking at a form**: a man of about twenty-six who carts salt tells a boy of about eleven holding a cart pole the trade, and the boy says the place back, and the man says the figure, and the boy says that then there was a great many at Coldharbour. A woman of about thirty-one with a second-hand stall has the printed slip in a box to keep pins in. A man of about thirty-three with a barrow puts a load down on the wrong side of a threshold because the man who ordered it has gone home. **The three have been in four counties for about twenty-nine days.** The ninth line is still ruled and empty, four hands have ruled it, the last of them a man of about twenty paid four pence to rule nine lines on a morning when there were eight words to put in them, and **it is empty because there is nothing in nine counties that will go in a line like that one.** **And the man of about thirty-two with a limp has a sheet of his own, three lines and no head, carrying the three days the things were entered on and one name against each, and it is the only record in the world of when and by whom, and nobody in that street knows he has it, and he is not going to give it to anybody.** The man of about thirty-three with a barrow comes through the street at about the tenth hour with a load of glass and a fold of paper in the inside pocket of a coat that is not his, and they go past each other at about the ninth pace, and neither says one word. **The last page is a morning and not a page of anybody's column.** Cost: **thirty-four pence**. Ending: decision.

### The ten endings, as a list

421 discovery · 422 discovery · 423 cost · 424 cost · 425 decision · 426 discovery · 427 discovery · 428 cost · 429 cost · 430 decision.

### What the block did not do, one line each, including the things it noticed and did not do

It did not name the antagonist, and it did not revive the Volume 08 cooperation. It did not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement, the budget being four of four and spent. It did not print the word that names the founding paper. It did not print *roof* or mention a coping's tile. It did not add an enemy, an office, a council, an order, a mechanism or a person with a want about any of this. **It did not make the place, the figure or the trade a lie or a forgery, and there is no villain to catch and nobody is brought to anything.** It did not correct the three and did not let the protagonist stop them. It did not have anybody find the bench-board or read it, and it did not print a word from it. It did not reconcile the refusal figures, add any two of them, or add any to a figure of persons. It did not move the count on the page under the fourth step and did not put a tenth asking to that bench. It did not print the marginal instruction, the count of the looks at a leaf, or the number of persons the office will not print on an offer. It did not explain the mechanism, make or schedule a cut, and it printed the volume's one System panel and no second. It did not resolve the shut road, the offer, the about eleven hundred, the two villages, the corridor's rule or its rate, or the want of either woman of about twenty-nine or thirty-nine, and it thanked nobody. It did not take Hushwater. It did not merge the three bars, and it touched only the bar four miles west of Marl. It did not answer the question from a road and did not have the keeper of records answer it, **and it noticed that the answer was nine miles from a drawer on the Monday it was standing in the same field, and it carried nothing back.** It did not soften the leak, un-say the ninth word, or have anybody regret it in a speech. It did not reprint the twenty-word engine sentence. It did not destroy or withdraw the register, repeal the quarantine, or adjourn the hearing. It did not improve on any of the four governances, or rank them, or make any of them lose. **It noticed that the bench that sits on Saturdays is the natural place for a thing people have to say out loud, and it did not take it there.** It did not spend Ivo Serrat's arithmetic before the identification, did not point at the mason's mark, and did not have anybody explain the ninth mark or ask the boy to read his book. It did not improve on a want.
