A boy of about sixteen puts two figures of about nine hundred people on one line of a sheet he was not supposed to be writing on, and the man of about forty-four tells him they are the same number and not one person is in both of them, and that a clerk nine counties off is going to add them. The man of about twenty-nine walks in at the eleventh hour with eight folded sheets he does not open, asks for the number in the mouths before the number on the page, names a rate on a road four counties off that nobody can check and will not put it in a column, and the man of about forty-four says out loud that a page with a price on it is a keeper and that the want he has been carrying with a coat off is the same want. **Column 98, the man of about forty-four's.**

**Ch 472 · the seventh of Thawmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market morning · the back gate of a salt factor's yard · nine people waiting to be hired to load a boat, and the block's central arithmetic, and a man of about thirty-two with a limp who says the other half of it and then stops talking.** The man of about twenty-nine works the sum out loud in the yard and says it twice. Nine cart-loads a day at three pence and about two hundred days is five thousand four hundred pence a year, and a widow at the eleventh house lives at twenty pence a day, and nine months of her winter is five thousand four hundred pence, and the request he has carried a month would take it. Aren Kest then says that the cost of it is not the shell and not the widow and not three pence a cart-load, and that the cost of it is that there will be a page, and that a shore keeping its own count in its own heads will have to start telling it to somebody, and that the somebody will be a man with a book and the man with the book decides what a cart-load of shell is worth. **Nobody improves on either. Column 106, the man of about twenty-nine's, his second.**

**Ch 473 · the twelfth of Thawmonth, YR 319, a Tuesday and not a market day · the ninth of eleven houses on the foreshore · what a salt wagon costs, who pays it, how long it takes, and the answer is nine months of somebody's winter.** The man of about fifty-three is named for the first time since 468 and the wagon is off the sand and tipping in nine minutes with the man of nine hundred acres standing in a doorway. He is paid three sacks a trip and the ninth house has paid nine pence a sack for eleven years, and that conversion is made once on the page and the salt is not in the money column. A day of that wagon costs fifty-four pence and earns twenty-seven, and about two hundred days a year makes that five thousand four hundred pence a year, which is two hundred and seventy days at twenty pence a day, which is nine months, every year, nineteen times, and nobody paid it and everybody did. The man with nine hundred acres goes inside nine seconds before the end of the second turn. A woman of about thirty-four with a barrow asks who he thinks paid it and he says he has never asked, and that a man who does not ask is a man who does not get to be angry, **and the exchange is printed in two turns of six and sixty-three, and the sixty-three is footed on the page, because a turn that is a second counting of the turn is the shape the cheapest defect in this block takes.** **Column 54, the man of about fifty-three's, the first he has written in nineteen years, and it is written on the back of a handcart docket with a nail.**

**Ch 474 · the eighteenth of Thawmonth, YR 319, a Monday and not a market day · a cart shed nine miles up a made road · a word, a song, and the coast's refusal turning out to have been right for a reason nobody gave out loud.** A woman of about twenty-four who has been at the pans says the word the working nine times in an hour, meaning four hours out of twelve when the water is off, and a carter of about forty says it back meaning a shift, and nine people at that shed turn round. She sings four lines of a pan song with a fifth line that came off a kiln four days off. The man of about twenty-nine says why the shore was right to refuse him, and says the true reason is a page. Aren Kest sits on the trough, asks her one question, is told not to be sorry, and is told not to be sorry because she would have said it herself in about four days. **Column 87, the man of about twenty-nine's, his fourth, and the four are still not joined up.**

**Ch 475 · the twenty-third of Thawmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · the seventh of eleven houses · a fever that came up the road in a wagon of shell, and the cost of nineteen years of a road nobody owns, and about nine hundred people and about nine hundred people in a room four days up a made road that are the same number and are never added.** Four people in the house nobody on that sand will name are lying down with a fever that arrived in nine cart-loads of shell and there is nowhere on that sand to send it and nobody to send it to. A woman of about thirty-four with a barrow works out inside two hours that a keeper would have given it an address in about nine days, says so out loud, says she is not turning about, and says the reason is that a man four days up that road is going to want to put a page on it and she has seen what a page does to a number. What happened between the sixth hour and the eleventh hour was the eleven houses: a pan, a fire that has not been out in forty-one years, a lease, a widow, and four of the eleven where a sick person can be taken. About four people found it out before the third hour and about nine hundred found out at once. **No list, no room, no meeting. Column 105, the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow's, the first she has written down.**

**Ch 476 · the twenty-eighth of Thawmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · a hundred paces from the landward end · the fifth time, and a different man, and four people who saw it.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet stands nine feet off the eleven paces of stone and writes for about twenty minutes. The second man is about twenty-eight, carries nothing, has no chain and no rod and no sheet, does not stand at the stone, and looks at the sand and then at the eleven houses one at a time from the landward end, and four people follow where he is looking without knowing they are doing it. A man of about fifty-three with a wagon does not follow him and has never once looked at his own sand from that end. The only conversation is a jug of water with a broken handle, in two turns, and the man of about thirty-four does not offer the second man any. **Nothing about the wall is scheduled and nobody asked the man of about thirty-four why he comes when he comes. Column 72, his, the fifth, and the second man's day is not in it because nobody asked him.**

**Ch 477 · the fifth of Mudmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · a street four hundred yards long in the market town · the ninth word, said nine times, and not one of the nine wrote it down.** A man of about forty with a cart says it at a trough to a boy of about fourteen holding a cart pole, in four printed turns, and the boy says there is a great many there. The man cannot say where he got it and there is a fifth line in a pan song and a slate that was written on and rubbed out. The woman of about fifty-eight with a slate writes the word yard and leaves the name off. A man of about nineteen at the bar hears the whole of it and was not asked and says nothing. The man of about twenty-nine says it last of the nine and does not write it down, and the reason he does not write it down is that he writes down things he can act on and he has four of them folded separately. Then a woman of about twenty-seven with a basket asks a man of about thirty-two with a limp what a road is and is told that you cannot get shut of one and that it is the second time in about two months somebody has asked him to, **and he is on his ninth day back in that town after nineteen days out of it, his five days in it at the start of Thawmonth having been the first of them.** **Column 103, the man of about nineteen at the bar's, the first one he has kept.**

**Ch 478 · the eighth of Mudmonth, YR 319, a Sunday and not a market day · one room over a weigh-house two hundred miles off that coast, with a door on a lane · a figure of about eleven hundred people, and a figure is not a question.** A band of about a hundred and four came back on the second day before and one sheet has about eleven hundred on it and no head on it. A woman of twenty-nine reads it standing up in about nine seconds and does not go to the press, and says out loud, twice, that a figure is not a question and that if she turns that number into a question she has made one she has to answer and she is not going to be the person who made it. She puts the sheet back into the band at the fourth place from the cord and ties the cord and writes the day on the outside of it in a hand a clerk nine counties off can read. A man of about twenty-three counts a hundred and thirty-two words on the steps and says he counted them because she made him wait. The register stands at seven questions and no findings, the day's book at nine, a drawer about eleven inches deep is not opened, and the about eleven hundred go back out in the band. **Column 122, hers, the fourth, and nobody thanked her.**

**Ch 479 · the fourteenth of Mudmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · the ninth of eleven houses on the foreshore · the protagonist's own arithmetic, and the block's turn, and a woman who is not a knight tells him he has been the reason a thing moved.** He has been adding days for two days and says the figures out loud: nine weeks is about a sixth of a year, a wagon year is five thousand four hundred pence the wrong way, a sixth of that is nine hundred pence, and nine hundred pence is forty-five days of the winter of a woman at the eleventh house. The woman of about thirty-four with a barrow walks nine hundred paces to tell him that the ninth house has not bought for nine weeks because a carter heard him talk about a keeper in the spring and then a man of about thirty-four came down the sand a fifth time with a different man, and a careful man stopped buying to find out whether the sand would still be there. He does not say it is not his fault. He says he said sixty-four words four days up a made road to one man and did not know there was a ninth house, **and that it was sixty-four words on a sheet with a fourth head on it that nobody has filled in, and that he counted them on the made road in about four days, and that he has not thought once in nine days that a sentence could do that, and that he was wrong about that and is not going to argue with her about it.** She then says the finding of the whole coast in ten words and asks whether the fourth head is still empty, and he says that it is, and she says she is not going to be thanked for it and neither is he. **He gets nothing and is not the keeper of anything. Column 82, Aren Kest's, the second he has kept, written standing up against a post with a nail.**

**Ch 480 · the nineteenth of Mudmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · a foreshore in the worst month for roads · the block's last page, and about four of them find out early and about nine hundred find out at once.** The water is off about four hours out of twelve and Mudmonth is not shorter than any other month; Mudmonth is soft, because the last half mile of the made road at the landward end is nine inches of black sand over a hard bank and a loaded cart that goes down in it does not come back on the flood. Three people say it out loud and a woman of sixty-three puts a third armful of wood on a fire that has not been out since a month in a year she does not give and says nothing. At the fifth hour a cart comes round the head of the bay with half a load on it and a man walking beside one wheel, and about nine hundred people find out at once. Nine cart-loads of shell still go off four boats into four pans, and a man of about fifty-three counts every one and sends no boat back empty and tells nobody why. **Aren Kest carries one armful of wood and is thanked for it by one woman and to nobody else. The piece of driftwood has twenty-two notches cut in the edge of it, one for each year, the twenty-second not cut, and the water did not go off that mark on the first of Thawmonth and did not go off it on the nineteenth of Mudmonth either. Column 96, the man of about twenty-three's.**

**The five chapters a Volume 10 Batch 0004 writer most often needs, and why each of them is one. Chapter 472** holds the block's finding in its plainest form, that a fixed price is a keeper, and it is the chapter Block 0004's wall is the answer to. **Chapter 473** holds the wagon's arithmetic at fifty-four against twenty-seven and the conversion of three sacks at nine pence, and every figure in the next block about what a road costs has to foot against it. **Chapter 475** holds the cost side of the coast's refusal, which is the thing a wall block must not forget. **Chapter 476** holds the different man with no instrument and the four people who followed where he looked, and that is the only page in the volume on which the wall's plan is visible as a plan. **Chapter 479** holds the price of a sentence, sixty-four words and nine weeks and nine hundred pence, and it is the chapter that says a man who talks about a keeper is a keeper, which is what the fourth block is about.

## Volume 10, Batch 0004 · Chapters 481 to 490

**Ch 481 · the twentieth day of Mudmonth, YR 319, a Friday and not a market day · at about a hundred paces from the landward end · the survey, a chain, four men who are not villains, and a sand that turns in about nine places.** A surveyor of about forty-four with a chain and a boy of about seventeen with a rod come down the sand for the ninth day of about nine, with a notebook with no head on any page, and the four men with them are a man of about thirty-eight with a crowbar, a man of about twenty-six with a shovel, a man of about fifty-one with a sledge and a man of about thirty-one with a barrow of sand, and not one of them is a knave and one of them says a sentence at the second hour and no other until the sixth. The sand turns in about nine places and none of the nine is a boundary anybody keeps, and the ninth is three black stones a storm put there in a month nobody now living was born in; a chain run straight across a turn comes up with a longer answer than the ground under it, and the surveyor has been walking round the nine instead of across them. **Aren Kest is standing on a rise of shell nine feet above the wet as the guest of a man of about fifty-three with a wagon and is not a party to anything; the chain comes over his sand twice and the boy sets the ring within four feet of his boot both times and nobody says anything to him either time.** The boy of about seventeen with the rod asks the man of about twenty-six with a shovel what a chain is for and is told it is for the hundred paces and that a number has to be got somehow, and a man of about forty-one who stands in the doorway of the seventh of the eleven houses is nine feet off and says it is the first time in nineteen years anybody has said out loud what a chain is for. **Column 91, the surveyor's, the second of about nine days.**

**Ch 482 · the twenty-sixth day of Mudmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the first floor over a chandler's shop in a market town of about two thousand people four days off · a deed that wants a signature and a form that wants a name, and the two are not the same piece of paper.** The deed is a foot and a half long, folded in three, with one ruled signature at the foot. The form has four heads cut into it, three filled in, the fourth empty since the middle of a month in Wolfmonth. A man of about thirty-four with a sheet comes four days up a made road, puts his own paper between them, and will not let a clerk of about forty-seven with the deed and a copyist of about thirty-one with the form touch each other's. He says the two are the same piece of work with the faces turned and that one office has been holding two of them for four months, and that the deed will get its signature inside nine days and the form will not get its name in nine days or nine years, and the reason is that the fourth head wants a person from two miles of sand and there is nobody on that sand who answers for a road. **A copyist of about thirty-one says out loud that the fourth head ought not to be on it and is right about it, and the clerk of about forty-seven says he has cut three of the four heads on about nine hundred forms in twenty-two years and has never cut that one; they agree in nothing and he is not a knave and she is not thanked.** He says out loud that he is not asking for the fourth head, and that nobody has taken up the offer of one sentence about the wall he made in about a hundred and ninety words in three turns on the twenty-ninth of a month in the year before this one, in eighty-six days. He then makes the longest speech of his in a room in a year and neither of the two men writes down all of it. **Nobody fills the fourth head and nobody says that anybody has filled it in anywhere else, and a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow on that sand asked whether it was still empty twelve days before and was not thanked. Column 118, his, the fourth.**

**Ch 483 · the twenty-eighth day of Mudmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · at the ninth of eleven houses · the ninth house buys again, and the price of the nine weeks is on the page in front of the people who paid it.** A wagon year is about two hundred days and twenty-seven pence a day the wrong way, which is five thousand four hundred pence a year, and the ninth house did not buy for nine weeks, and a sixth of five thousand four hundred is nine hundred pence. The figure was worked out in about nine seconds by a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow in a market town four days off on the fourteenth of this month, went back on a cart, and has been on the ninth house about a fortnight. A man with nine hundred acres inland comes out onto his own step for the first time in nine weeks and asks for the figure not to be said on the sand. **He buys three sacks at nine pence a sack as he has for eleven years, and he says he did not stop buying because of the man with the sheet but because a wall of stone appeared at a hundred paces from the landward end of a road with no line on it, and that the two of them have an arrangement about nine weeks old that is not on a docket with a nail on it.** The man of about fifty-three counts the sacks out loud and stops before the third. **Column 76, his, the second he has written in nineteen years, and nine hundred pence and three sacks at nine pence are not in it.**

**Ch 484 · the second ebb after the one where a boy of about seventeen with a rod asked what a chain was for · at the boat land at the landward end · a boat of glass, and what a chain is for and what it is not for.** A flat-bottomed punt is on the shingle and a man of about thirty-three with a barrow is taking glass out of it. A boy of about seventeen with the rod has a chain laid flat on the wet sand about forty yards long with a slack in the middle because the middle of it is soft, and the man with the barrow tells him that a chain is not for deciding where anything goes and is only for finding out how far one thing is from another, and that a chain is a stick you lay on the ground to see how big the ground is and the ground is there either way. **A man of about fifty-three with a wagon has to step over the chain twice and says it will be under the water at about the ninth hour, to nobody, and is right, and nobody rolls the chain up and nine people step over the slack and not over the handles. Aren Kest is the only other person on that shingle carrying nothing and neither he nor the wagon man was the reason the other one was there. Column 134, the boy's, the first he has ever kept, on the back of a rod-measuring sheet with a pencil stub out of a shed.**

**Ch 485 · the eleventh day of Greenmonth, YR 319, a Friday and not a market day · from the wall to the eleventh of eleven houses · four hundred yards, and no paper joining them.** A surveyor of about forty-four runs a chain from the landward face of eleven paces of stone to the door of the eleventh of eleven houses and gets four hundred yards, and the chain goes slack in the middle twice, and he writes four hundred yards on a page with no head on it and turns the page over. A boy of about seventeen with the rod says *that is a long way* and is told *it is a number, it does not mean that anything is between them*. **The wall is not a boundary, not a toll, not a gate, not a rate and not a keeper, it has not stopped a cart in the two months it has been there, and no man has named a way it is against anything.** A man of about forty-one who stands in the doorway of the seventh says he had known the distance would be about that and had not wanted it written down. **A widow of about seventy at the eleventh house was not in and was not told and bought four sacks at nine pence a sack and will not sell them back; four of the eleven houses and seven of them are two hundred paces from that wall and are never added to eleven in a column. Aren Kest stands two hundred paces seaward of the wall all day and goes to none of the three houses. Column 85, the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow's, the second.**

**Ch 486 · the seventeenth day of Greenmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · nine paces off the wall · the sixth time, and the question nobody asked.** A man of about thirty-four with a sheet stands nine paces off eleven paces of stone with a board and has not written on it, and nine people are inside nine feet of him all doing something else. **A woman of about thirty-four with a barrow asks why he comes when he comes in seven words, and he says he is sent, that a man in a room with a seal decides how often the place is looked at, that he has never been told who and is never going to ask, and that if he asks he is a man who is asking instead of a man who is looking at a wall.** He then says the only thing about his six visits that is his own is that he has not found out whether the fourth head wants a person or wants a word, and that there is a room of nine where the answer probably is and he is not going to look for it, and she says that is six times of a man not looking and he says it back. **The answer is not a conspiracy and it is not nothing, and nothing in this chapter says whose plan the wall is, and the second man of about twenty-eight was not on the sand and nobody looked for him.** A man of about fifty-three with a wagon was at the seventh and did not hear any of it, and a woman of sixty-three put a fifth armful of wood on a fire that has not been out in forty-one years. **Column 109, his, the sixth, kept in a fold in a coat and never joined up.**

**Ch 487 · the third ebb after the one where a man of about thirty-four with a sheet was asked why he comes · at the landward end, two hundred paces short of it · two instruments at the same hour that do not touch.** Four of the eleven houses have somebody's work happening in them and seven do not, and at a hundred paces from the landward end four men and a boy of about seventeen with a rod are working on eleven paces of stone with a chain that went slack twice twelve days back. **About nine hundred people cross both of them between the second hour and the sixth hour, and a yard keeps its count in days and a road keeps its count in tides and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and none of the three was added to another by anybody.** Nine cart-loads of shell go off four boats into four pans. The only conversation between the two instruments is about a barrow, between the man of about thirty-eight with a crowbar and the man of about thirty-three with a barrow, and it ends with each of them saying he has a wall and he has a boat and that this is two men on sand and not an arrangement. **Aren Kest is two hundred paces seaward at the fifth hour, sees both at once, is not a keeper of either, says one sentence to a man of about twenty-three who rows one of the four boats about the slack in the middle of the chain, and is told the slack was the only honest part of the morning. Column 143, the man of about thirty-three with a barrow's, the third.**

**Ch 488 · the twenty-ninth day of Greenmonth, YR 319, a Tuesday and not a market day · four hundred yards up the sand from the landward end · a season in money at one house and a season in something else at another.** A season on that sand is the days the water is off long enough, and a wagon year is about two hundred days. A widow of about seventy at the eleventh house does not have a season; she has days, and twenty pence a day, and two hundred days at twenty pence a day is four thousand pence. **She says she could not tell you how many days there are in a year and neither could he, and he has just got a number for it and thinks she does not know that.** At the ninth house, which has a lease and about nine hundred acres inland behind it, the cost of the same about two hundred days is two carts up the made road, four days of a boy's legs each way, and a question the man with the lease asked out loud in front of nine people that he had said every week for nineteen years and had never once had to say: whether he would buy off a wagon that comes twice a day. **The two figures are on the same stretch of sand, one is money and one is not, they are not added, and there is no rate in nine counties that converts a boy's two legs into pence there.** Aren Kest hears the first two turns and writes both figures on the back of a handcart docket at somebody else's fire and says neither out loud. **Column 94, the woman of about thirty-four with a barrow's, the fourth, and the two figures are not in it.**

**Ch 489 · the first day of Longlight, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · at the seaward end · a month carried at thirty begins and there is nothing on that sand to swear.** Longlight is when boundaries are sworn, and a field about four miles off a made road has a man with a mallet putting a figure into a hedge-stone, and the sand turns in about nine places and none of the nine is a boundary anybody keeps and the ninth of them is three black stones a storm put there. **The oath-stone four miles off is missing and no second swearing has been set for it and nobody has been sent, and a foreshore has nothing to do with it and is not going to be told about it.** About nine hundred people cross the best month of the year and find out that nothing has changed. A woman of about fifty-one looks at a mark on a driven post for as long as it takes a man to say nine and goes away, and the twenty-second notch is not cut. **The only thing anybody says out loud about the first of a month is a woman of about twenty-seven with a basket saying in fourteen words that there is nothing here to swear, which is the third time she has said it and the fourth person she has said it to, and she is not asking.** **Column 127, hers, the first she has ever kept, on the back of a fish slat with a nail.**

**Ch 490 · the first day of Longlight, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day, at the ninth hour and the eleventh hour and the second hour of the afternoon · the block's last page.** Eleven paces of stone about two feet high with a second footing course nine inches lower takes the water over it for the second time that day with Aren Kest standing nine paces off on the seaward side, not asked to be there and not asked anything. **About four hundred yards of sand runs from the landward face of that wall to the door of the eleventh of eleven houses and a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow walks all of it at the eleventh hour and comes back along the tide line, and she is the only person who has walked the whole of it in either direction since the chain was run, and she says she has not written a line on it and is not going to and is not going to tell a man of about forty-one with a basket that she walked it.** The wall is not a boundary, not a toll, not a gate, not a rate and not a keeper, it has never stopped a cart, and nobody in nine counties has named a way it is against anything. **A man of about fifty-three with a wagon goes down on the ebb, stands about two hours and comes back on the flood, and the ninth of eleven buys three sacks at nine pence a sack as it has for eleven years; the fourth head is empty; nobody has taken up the offer of one sentence about the wall; about four hundred yards of sand is not a gap in anything, it is four hundred yards; and both the wall and the four hundred yards are still there.** **Column 100, Aren Kest's, the fifth, and he is not a keeper of either of them and walks back up the sand on his own.**

**The five chapters a Volume 10 Batch 0005 writer most often needs.** **Chapter 482** holds the two documents and the fourth head and the only person in nine counties who has cut the other three, and it is the chapter a volume close has to argue with. **Chapter 486** holds the answer that is neither a conspiracy and not nothing, and it is the only place in the volume where a man who comes down a sand says why he comes. **Chapter 487** holds the two instruments running at once without touching, and it is the argument the volume's conclusion is a consequence of. **Chapter 488** holds the two seasonal costs, four thousand pence at one house and two carts at another, and the rule that they are never added. **Chapter 490** holds the last page and the thing a close must not summarise: a wall four hundred yards from a house, both still there.

**What a review repair changed in these ten chapters, and nothing else did.** Four money columns were re-voiced — 134 in 484, 85 in 485, 109 in 486 and 143 in 487 — with every price, every term, every printed sum-list entry and every total exactly as they were, because a re-voice may not move a fact. Two dateline clauses were re-voiced, the market-town clause in 482 and the salt-wagon clause in 483, and the wagon in 483 is now named as the man of about fifty-three's, which is who drives it. Four clauses were re-voiced off the retired reporting frame, in 484, 486 and 487, and the people and the judgments in them are the same people and the same judgments. **No quoted speech was touched in any of the ten chapters, so every printed speech-length figure in this block still foots against the speech it is attached to.** The per-chapter word counts as the block left them and as the files stand are 1,965 / 2,326 / 1,896 / 2,155 / 2,072 / 1,916 / 2,230 / 1,933 / 1,984 / 1,880 and 1,965 / 2,326 / 1,897 / 2,160 / 2,075 / 1,922 / 2,224 / 1,933 / 1,984 / 1,880.

## Volume 10, Batch 0005 — Chapters 491 to 500, the last block of the volume

**Batch 0001 · Ch 451–460 · Block 0002 · Ch 461–470 · Block 0003 · Ch 471–480 · Block 0004 · Ch 481–490 · Block 0005 · Ch 491–500 · Volume 10 is complete**

**Ch 491 · the eighth day of Longlight, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · at the landward end, a hundred paces in · the fourth and last of four clauses about a wagon, and the fourth is the only one of the four that is a sentence about what happens afterwards.** A wagon comes off the sand with a near wheel in the channel and a man of about thirty-three with a barrow gets it up in about as long as it takes a man to say nine, and nobody calls him, nobody pays him, and nobody asks him how it went. The sentence is **nobody asks you how it went**, and it is carried at two ends of that sand on this tide and cannot be carried at the third, because the port four days off pays about nine hundred people twice in seven days and a man standing in a weigh-house cannot say four clauses out loud without making three of them a lie. **Aren Kest is four hundred yards up the sand, the hundred and fourth person over it that day, and is not the reason any of it works.** Three exchanges: 48 words in four turns, 62 in four, 173 in five. Column 120 pence, the man of about fifty-three's third in nineteen years.

**Ch 492 · the fifteenth day of Longlight, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · behind a weigh-house at the landward end, in one low room · three rooms in one building for the second time in a hundred and fifty-one days, and a century that is a round figure and not a fact.** A man of about sixty with a slipway has been saying *about a century* about the floor and the beams for forty-one years and a man four days up says it could be either, and the man of sixty did not thank him and did not tell him he was right. **Aren Kest says out loud that he is not the reason the three rooms are in that building and was not thanked for saying it, because saying it was not a service**, and he came down for a tide and has no business here. A woman of about thirty-four with a barrow shuts the door behind her. Four exchanges: 84 in six turns, 93 in seven, 207 in six, 99 in three. Column 99 pence, the man of about thirty-four's fifth up and down a made road.

**Ch 493 · the twenty-sixth day of Longlight, YR 319, a Monday and not a market day, four days before the last of the month · a hundred paces off the landward end · four men shoring the landward face of eleven paces of stone, and none of them is a knave.** A woman of about thirty-four with a barrow tells a man of about thirty-eight with a crowbar he is putting timber against the wrong face, and he is not, and neither of them is being difficult. A boy of about seventeen with a rod keeps the day's column on the back of a rod-measuring sheet and says *I do not put your words in my book* and writes nothing on the other side. Two exchanges: 129 words in seven turns, and 32 in one turn in two spans of five and twenty-seven. Column 142 pence, the boy's fourth ever kept.

**Ch 494 · the thirteenth day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the first ebb after the night · four of the eleven houses lose the ground under the seaward wall of them and none of the four is lost.** Eleven people put it back in courses that are not level in about nine days without anybody calling them and without anybody being paid. **Aren Kest is the sixth one over, carried a basket for about four days, and is not the reason any of the four houses was got back, and a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow says so to a man of about thirty-one with a barrow of sand and thirty feet off him.** A woman of sixty-three tells a man of about thirty-four with a sheet that he has written nine and the sea puts a bank back in eleven, and shuts the door about a foot and a half. Two exchanges: 107 words in five turns, 64 in four. Column 117 pence, the woman's fifth.

**Ch 495 · the seventeenth day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Monday and not a market day · the fourth hour after the water went out, at the seaward end · the coast's own paper, and the fourth head is filled, and what is in it is a word and not a person.** Three terms are read out four times on a foreshore where nobody has said a day of the week in about nine years. The sheet is a foot and a half long with four heads cut into it: a place, a figure of houses, a trade, and a word that is the coast's name for the road. **A man of about forty-one with a basket asked, after nine years of carrying baskets over that road, whether there is a keeper, and was told there is not one, and was not thanked for asking.** The man of about thirty-four from the port four days off said the sentence about a word and a name and took it back off himself in the next turn. Three exchanges: 139 words in four turns, 119 in five, 249 in six. Column 92 pence, the woman's first.

**Ch 496 · the twenty-first day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Friday and not a market day · in one low room whose beams come down to about four feet above a stone floor, behind a weigh-house · the climax, and it is a no, and the reason is not a principle.** Asked whether his road and the coast's history can be made to be one account, Aren Kest says no in one word, is asked to say it again, says it again in the same word **and in the accent of that shore, knowing he is doing it**, and then gives nine reasons, which are that a road with a beginning is written in days and a coast's history cut in driftwood is written in years, and that a merged account forces one of them to be a keeper, and a keeper is a person, and a person can be asked. Three exchanges: 42 words in four turns, 444 in eighteen, 222 in four. Column 138 pence, his sixth, the first he has written standing up.

**Ch 497 · the twenty-second day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · from the landward end to two hundred paces short of the seaward end · the cost of the no, spoken by somebody other than him.** The man of about thirty-four from the port four days off goes up the sand to ask the woman of about fifty-one for the driftwood in her post and **she says no in one word and does not say why, and he writes two letters on a sheet with no head on it and carries them up a made road without telling anybody at the landward end.** At about the ninth hour a man of about fifty-three with a wagon says out loud, in front of about four hundred people, what the asking cost, and a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow says one word to him and would not say it twice. Two exchanges: 195 words in eight turns, 255 in five. Column 156 pence, the man of about twenty-seven with four boats, his first, kept on the back of a thwart.

**Ch 498 · the twenty-third day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Sunday and not a market day · at the landward end and at the fourth, ninth and eleventh of eleven houses · the first durable multi-path institution in the book, and it has no keeper and no fixed day.** A sheet on a flat stone at the landward end and about four mouths in four counties. **No meeting, no room, nobody called anybody, and the three rooms that have each said a thing out loud have not said it together and have not fixed a day to say it again.** A woman of sixty-three who has sat behind a pan for forty-one years says out loud for the first time in that time that she has not opened anything and has not shut anything. A man of about thirty-eight with a crowbar comes nine hundred yards to say seven words nobody asked him for: **do not put it on a form, and a form is a thing that finds out.** Three exchanges: 154 words in seven turns, 203 in six, 87 in three. Column 151 pence, the woman's first, kept on the back of a fish slat with a nail.

**Ch 499 · the twenty-fourth day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Monday and not a market day · a bar, a yard of nine carts and a locked room in a town of about four thousand people in the fen county · five paths, and thirty-one nights against thirty-four, and a difference of three.** A woman of about sixty-two who keeps a bar asks where he sleeps and is told *it depends*, and is not thanked and does not ask again. A man of about forty-four with a horseshoe tells him that nobody upends the board and everybody writes on it, and that **a man who does not know which of his two numbers is true has one number and a gap, and the gap is where the three nights are.** A woman of about thirty-four with a barrow in another yard, who is not the woman who carried the sheet, says eleven words about watching him get his weight off his leg and goes out the far side without waiting. The room over the yard stays locked on purpose and the four pence is not taken and is not offered. Four exchanges: 281 words in nine turns, 222 in six, 29 in three, 197 in five. Column 125 pence, the horseshoe man's fourth, the first three with a head on it and this one without.

**Ch 500 · the twenty-fifth day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Tuesday and not a market day · a room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane, and then a kitchen · the last page.** A chipped bowl on a shelf above the pan instead of inside the press, a hook with a piece of horse-brass on it on the third nail from the left instead of the fifth, and a stool under a window that does not shut and never did. **He was not sure of the room and he placed it anyway, and the reason is that the bowl and the hook and the stool are his and the room is not, and this is the second placing of that room in the volume after Chapter 459.** He opens nothing, closes nothing and signs nothing. A woman of about sixty-three on a coast two hundred miles off and a man of about thirty-two in a room answer each other in three turns and neither knew the other had said anything, and the deed is not called good by anybody and is not called bad by anybody. **The wall is a hundred paces from the landward end and four hundred yards is still four hundred yards, and five paths are open and none of them has a keeper, and he cannot say which of his days belong to Merefen and has stopped trying to say it out loud.** Two exchanges: 109 words in three turns, and 54 in three turns of an exchange that did not happen. Column 139 pence, the man of thirty-four's sixth.

**The ten chapters a Volume 11 writer most often needs.** **Chapter 496** holds the no and the nine reasons and the accent, and it is the volume's climax. **Chapter 495** holds the three terms and the fourth head and the word. **Chapter 498** holds the institution and the eleven words' thirty-four-word rule and the sheet on the stone. **Chapter 499** holds the five roads and the difference of three. **Chapter 500** holds the kitchen and the two unreconciled counts and the last page.

**Nothing else in these ten chapters was changed after the first printing except by the re-voicing recorded in `state/batch-summaries.md`, and no chapter of Volumes 01 to 09 was edited.**

## Volume 11, Batch 0001 — Chapters 501 to 510, "A Return With Four Heads"

**Days 1381 to 1434, the twenty-sixth of Rainmonth YR 319 to the nineteenth of Fallowmonth YR 319, a Wednesday to a Sunday, fifty-four days inclusive, eight market mornings. Every date in the block is YR 319 and the year turns on day 1596, which is Block 4 and not this one. Block 0001 is 20,943 words in ten chapters, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, and the manuscript is 1,504,444 in five hundred and ten chapters. The block's argument in one sentence: *a form that asks a road about itself has told somebody where the road is, and the telling is the finding-out, and the fourth head is empty in nine counties and nobody can say who cut it.* No crossing is conducted in this block or in this volume, nothing is opened, no System panel appears, no stage or ledger or qualification word appears, the antagonist is not named, and two of the volume's five names are spent.**

**Ch 501 · the twenty-sixth day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Wednesday and not a market day · outside a weigh-house with a board on it, in a market town of about two thousand people four days up a made road from a coast · two sheets on one board, and a return is not a notice, and about four of nine people who copied an address do not know which of the two they copied.** The return asks for **a place, a figure of people, and a trade**, and has a fourth head cut into it and ruled and empty, and says at the bottom *Nobody is to be sent to find out*. A man of about forty-four with a stall reads both sheets twice and says he does not know which he is waiting for, and a woman of about thirty-one tells him he will lose a day's takings whichever it is. **A man of about thirty-eight with a satchel comes at the fifth hour to check the date, finds it correct, says so, and does not pick up the sheet underneath.** The fourth head has been empty on every return that has come back and nobody in that town can say who cut it. One exchange: 180 words in seven turns. Column 74 pence, the man of about twenty-nine who puts up notices, the fourth board this month.

**Ch 502 · the twenty-ninth day of Rainmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane about two hundred miles up a made road · a rider comes eleven miles with a letter of four sentences, and the third of them asks for fifty sheets.** **Mara Vey, twenty-nine, four years and a half, fourpence a year, out of a deed of thirteen words.** She refuses the fifty and sends **the four heads** instead, on her own paper, with a sentence of fourteen words under the fourth: *the fourth head is empty here too and I have not been told why.* **A woman of about thirty-one with a pen wrote the heads out with a straightedge and a knife and did not ask a second question, and Mara Vey was not thanked and did not say thank you.** The woman of about thirty-one with a pen then puts a *complaint* under the fourth head, and is told that a complaint is a thing somebody can answer. Three exchanges: 2 words in two turns, 123 in three, 119 in four. Column 80 pence, the sixth she has kept in four and a half years.

**Ch 503 · the fourth day of Harvestmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · Gault Prentice, about fifty-one, in a grey coat, comes up the lane on foot with a satchel and does not send a rider.** He says his own name once and does not say whose plan it is. He is right about the danger a controlled disclosure is built against: a man of about thirty-eight with a satchel copied a return out of that room on the first of a month in the year before this one, at a table by the door, in about nine minutes, at no charge, and nobody stopped him. **Aren Kest, thirty-two, with a limp, is in the room from the third hour, has read about four hundred and nine returns in two days, says about nine sentences, and three of them are the name of a road.** He has not been in a room with Mara Vey since a Tuesday in Rainmonth in the year before this one, there is a thing between them that neither has said in either direction, and **it is not said here.** Four exchanges: 141 words in four turns, 269 in eight, 173 in five, 62 in five. Column 86 pence, the first one he has kept in about four months, and a man's column and not an office's.

**Ch 504 · the sixth day of Harvestmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · a hired room over a saddler's · the first sitting, and the chain of office is a badge and not an instrument.** About nine people in the room and about four hundred in the town. **Nesta Braith, twenty-six, a copyist, rules four lines with the same knife and the same rule and will not say a thing twice in two counties in a month.** A man of about fifty-eight asks what the fourth head is for and gets *nobody knows* from a woman who was not asked. A man of about twenty-nine who keeps an inn and has a tally-stick gives **six hundred and eight** — the carts that came down that street on the last market morning — and the clerk of about twenty-two writes it under a head that says *a figure of people*, and it is still on the paper. Two exchanges: 69 words in six turns, 138 in five. Column 90 pence, the first of about nine hundred a year.

**Ch 505 · the eleventh day of Harvestmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · five returns out of nine counties, and one of them has had the fourth head cut out with a knife.** Four of the five came back whole with the same four heads cut in the same wood by four different knives. **A county is not a man, says a woman of about thirty-four, and the man who cut the line out is not a villain and does not know his form is on a table four days up a made road.** A fifth return has come from a coast four days off with no date on it, because nobody on that coast writes a month or a day. Two exchanges: 189 words in nine turns, 208 in five. Column 95 pence, the man of about thirty-eight's ninth in nine years.

**Ch 506 · the thirteenth day of Harvestmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · the room over the weigh-house · nine words and then four sentences, and then a second set of four ruled heads in a second county.** **Controlled disclosure is another kind of the same silence.** A room is not the people who are in it, and it is not smaller when you count the people who are in it, and it is not smaller because you have written four names at the top of a sheet. The other three sentences are about what would have to be done to the sheet, and the third of them is the only one of the four that is about the woman who says it, and it says that if she did it she would tell the woman of about thirty-one with a pen in about four months and before anybody else. **The second set of four heads is ruled a little tighter than the first, deliberately, and the reason is given and is that a wider line looks like a space for a word.** Three exchanges: 342 words in eight turns, 312 in seven, 149 in four. Column 101 pence, the seventh she has kept.

**Ch 507 · the eighteenth day of Harvestmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · the real question, which is not how many roads there are.** *How many roads can be written on a return, without somebody walking them?* About ninety returns have come in under the wrong question. **The man of about fifty-eight takes the chain of office off and puts it on the table and says the thing he has made is worse than he thought, and will take the notice down on Saturday in the room and not in the street.** Two returns from that coast carry the same word under the first head, the same about nine hundred under the second, four trades under the third, and an empty fourth, and a man of about thirty-eight with a satchel says write down both. **The room decides that a clerk goes down a road rather than a road being asked, and the form in front of them says at the bottom that nobody is to be sent to find out.** Two exchanges: 248 words in six turns, 127 in four. Column 107 pence, the man's second in about a month.

**Ch 508 · the twenty-fifth day of Harvestmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · a return goes out with about four hundred against it, and the four hundred are the people who were in a lane on a Tuesday.** A man of about forty-one with a cart walked the lane on the Tuesday, filled the form on the Wednesday, and handed it in on the Thursday, and said one sentence on his way out: **that is the figure of the people who were in the lane on the Tuesday, not the ones who live there.** A woman of about thirty-four says put no figure and put his sentence under the second head instead, **and the clerk puts the figure in and does not put the sentence, and does not leave the head empty either, and the copyist noticed and said nothing.** A clerk of about forty-four who has been copying for about nineteen years copies the whole of it, fourth head included. Two exchanges: 56 words in one turn in two spans, 159 in five, 9 in two. Column 110 pence, the man of about forty-one's fourth in about four months.

**Ch 509 · the second day of Fallowmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · a woman of about twenty-seven walks up the lane with a copy of the return, and the cost is two words spoken by a man of about fifty-eight.** She is not angry. She reads about a hundred and seven words of the form out loud and says she is not angry about the counting and is angry about the line, and **the copyist says *I ruled it* from the side table without getting up.** **It works**, says the man of about fifty-eight, and then says four sentences about what to do about it, and a man of about thirty-eight with a satchel says the word for what he has described and is not answered. In the week after, nine counties sent back about nine returns each where four counties had sent about nine. Two exchanges: 201 words in eight turns, 198 in four. Column 116 pence, the woman's first, kept on the back of a saddler's receipt.

**Ch 510 · the nineteenth day of Fallowmonth, YR 319, a Sunday and not a market day · the same room, shut, with about four people in it because the cart for a weigh-house two hundred miles off goes on Mondays and comes back on Sundays · the block's end, and what changes is that a man of thirty-two with a limp is a person a room can write down.** The copyist will not say it again and says what she is going to do with the fourth head instead: **rule it a sixteenth of an inch narrower in the second county than in the first, so that a word put in it in one will not fit in the other, and a man in four years with nine counties of forms can tell which county a word was written in without being told.** A woman of about thirty-four from a weigh-house asks *can a form be stopped* and Aren Kest says he has stopped one, once, on a false witness, and that about nine people on the other side were never told, and that a thing which happened once is not a promise. **The man of about fifty-eight writes his name in the back of a book of carts at about the eleventh hour and does not read it out, and the copyist saw and said nothing and was right to.** Two exchanges: 170 words in six turns, 183 in six. Column 123 pence, his seventh since the twenty-fifth of a month carried at thirty in the year before this one.

**The ten chapters a Volume 11 writer most often needs.** **Chapter 502** holds the fourteen words under the fourth head and the refusal of the fifty. **Chapter 503** holds the controlled disclosure and the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel. **Chapter 505** holds the five returns and the cut-out line. **Chapter 506** holds the nine words and the second set of four heads. **Chapter 509** holds the two words that say the form works. **Chapter 510** holds the sixteenth of an inch and the answer that is not a no.

**Nothing in these ten chapters was changed after the first printing except by the re-voicing and the numeric repairs recorded in `state/batch-summaries.md`, and no chapter of Volumes 01 to 10 was edited.**

## Volume 11, Batch 0002 — Chapters 511 to 520, "The Copies and the Cost of a Sample"

**Days 1438 to 1488, the twenty-third of Fallowmonth YR 319 to the thirteenth of Wolfmonth YR 319, a Thursday to a Friday, fifty-one days inclusive, six market mornings. Every date in the block is YR 319 and the year turns on day 1596, which is Block 4 and not this one. Block 0002 is 20,938 words in ten chapters after the review repair, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, and the manuscript is 1,525,382 in five hundred and twenty chapters, and both figures are the recount and not the first printing. The block's argument in one sentence: *a copy of a form is the only form nobody has ever regulated, and two copies of one return now disagree about a place, and both of them are right, and a person can be found at one of them and a road cannot.* No crossing is conducted in this block or in this volume, nothing is opened, no System panel appears, no stage or ledger or qualification word appears, the antagonist is not named, no name is spent, and the word that names the founding paper is printed nowhere in it.**

**Ch 511 · the twenty-third day of Fallowmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · a hired room over a saddler's · six hundred and eight and five hundred and seventy-one, and a man of about twenty-nine who has been counting carts for six years.** He keeps two tally-sticks, one for the Saturday market and one for the Thursday market, and he worked that out by himself and has never been told it was right. **He asks what the number is for and the answer is printed on the page and it is that the room does not know, said kindly, and the man of about fifty-eight writes it in the back of a book of carts so that the next man does not have to ask.** Two figures of carts are two counts of two different mornings and go on a bare leaf with no head over them. Four exchanges: 203 words in four turns, 80 in two, 163 in eight, 63 in one turn. Column 81 pence, the man of about twenty-nine's sixth, kept on a tally-stick.

**Ch 512 · the twenty-seventh day of Fallowmonth, YR 319, a Monday and not a market day · the same room · a fifth line, ruled by a clerk and cut off by a copyist, and the two counties are two counties.** A clerk of about forty-six with grey in her hair, from the county two hundred miles off, ruled a fifth line on one of the four ruled heads because the book in her county has five columns and the sheet has four. **The finding is that a copy is not a copy once somebody has added a line to it, and the added line has no head on it, and nobody may fill it and nobody may say anybody has.** Nesta Braith takes the line off in about four minutes with the knife she rules with. Three exchanges: 116 words in five turns, 226 in six, 110 in three. Column 93 pence, the clerk's first, on the back of a return.

**Ch 513 · the second day of Embermonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · the same room · Gault Prentice's compromise, in nine sentences, and he says first that it is worse than the offer he made before.** He offers to send the four names of the four readers to the nine counties in a letter so that a road may complain to a person. **He would rather be found than be right, and the room does not agree with him and about four of the nine think he is right, and the letter goes back into his coat at about the seventh hour with the four names still off it.** Three exchanges: 312 words in nine sentences in one turn, 149 in four, 81 in three. Column 102 pence, his second in about four months, and a man's column and not an office's.

**Ch 514 · the twenty-first day of Embermonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · a page up a made road after nineteen days with three sentences on it that do not answer the question.** **A place is not a road until somebody has been asked to call it one; a figure is not a figure of people until somebody has said which hour; a road that can complain to a person is a road that has been found, and the finding is the thing the road was trying to avoid.** **A letter is a form that walks**, said by the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel, and a wall is about half of the answer. Nobody is thanked. Three exchanges: 106 words in three sentences in one turn, 182 in six, 143 in four. Column 115 pence, the keeper of the room over the weigh-house's eighth, in the same book as the seventh, and this room did not see it.

**Ch 515 · the twenty-eighth day of Embermonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · a third return from a coast four days off, reported and not present.** The word under the first head is the same word, the figure under the second is the same figure, four trades under the third, the fourth head empty, three dates nine days apart that are all the day the boat came. **The copyist copied the word in nine minutes instead of four and said out loud that her hand had decided before she did.** A Crown form and a sheet on a flat stone are two documents and the paragraph says so. Three exchanges: 206 words in six turns, 192 in five, 101 in four. Column 124 pence, the copyist's first, in a pocket.

**Ch 516 · the thirtieth day of Embermonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · a made lane about a mile and a half, ending at eleven houses and a chapel · two words for one lane, and the form asked for one.** At the rail of a common four people call it the Green Lane and at the eleventh of eleven houses six people call it the Long Lane, and both of them live at the end of it. **A place has more names than a form has lines, and a form that asks for one of them gets whichever the person filling it in already knew.** Nobody turned it into a boundary and nobody chose. Two exchanges: 97 words in five turns, 212 in five. Column 129 pence, the clerk of about thirty-four's ninth in eleven years and the first with a lane in it.

**Ch 517 · the fifth day of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · a woman of about thirty-four copies a return in about nine minutes at a table in her own kitchen and asks whether it is allowed.** She is told that it is not allowed and is not forbidden and that those are two different sentences, and that **there is no head anywhere in nine counties for a copy.** She brings a second sheet, and the two sheets disagree about a place, and about four hundred people at the end of that lane have each got a copy and about nine have compared them. **A copy of a form finds out later, and in a different direction, and by a longer road, and nobody has to sign anything.** Four exchanges: 149 words in three turns, 210 in five, 200 in five, 47 in three. Column 133 pence, her first, on the back of the bad copy.

**Ch 518 · the eighth day of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Sunday and not a market day · the same room, shut on Sundays except on a Sunday the cart comes back, and it came back that morning, with about nine people in it · one word in a fourth head, the first in nine counties in about four years. **The woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key said at the door that whoever came in on that Sunday would be washing the floor of that room afterwards and nobody else, and the floor has not been washed.**** It came from the county whose return came back in Harvestmonth with the fourth head cut out with a knife. **A woman of about fifty-three who takes in washing says out loud that she was told to fill it in and that she wanted the same head stopped, and both of those are true, and nobody asked her by whom and nobody asked her why, and the man of about fifty-eight was told twice by two different people in front of the other seven that he had asked the wrong question.** The page says on the face of it that the rule that the fourth head stays empty was never the office's to keep. Two exchanges: 150 words in six turns, 158 in five. Column 140 pence, her first, on a washing board.

**Ch 519 · the ninth day of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Monday and not a market day · the same room · the second cost, spoken by somebody other than him, and the first refusal nobody argued with.** A clerk of about twenty-two is asked for a book that is not the room's book and says no in nine words. The man of about fifty-eight is told that he counted nine market mornings in it and there are eight and the ninth is the four saddles, and he says he will take the nine out of his own book and is told he will not. **A man of about fifty-one in a grey coat says what it costs: a room with a thing in it that the room does not have is a room that cannot be asked.** Four exchanges: 125 words in four turns, 119 in five, 154 in six, 195 in two. Column 149 pence, the clerk of about twenty-two's first, on the back of a leaf that is not one of the nine.

**Ch 520 · the thirteenth day of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Friday and not a market day · the same room · the block's end, and it is not a decision and it is not a resolution.** A fifth line that nobody ordered went up the made road in the Monday bundle. **Two copies of one return are in two counties and they do not agree about the first line, and a man of about fifty-three with a barrow can be found at the rail of a common and a woman of about twenty-seven can be found at the eleventh of eleven houses, and the road cannot be found at either.** The fourth head on the Crown's form on the table is empty and the word in the fourth head of a returned form belongs to a woman of about fifty-three who takes in washing, **and those are two documents and one of them is not the form.** The hearing stands. Two exchanges: 144 words in four turns, 256 in three. Column 153 pence, the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel's tenth in about nine years.

**The ten chapters a Volume 11 writer most often needs.** **Chapter 512** holds the fifth line and the copy that is not a copy. **Chapter 513** holds the nine sentences and the letter that was not sent. **Chapter 514** holds the three sentences that do not answer the question. **Chapter 516** holds the two words for one lane and the finding about a place having more names than a form has lines. **Chapter 518** holds the one word in the fourth head and the two acts by two people in one county. **Chapter 520** holds the person who can be found and the road that cannot.

**The ten chapters were changed after the first printing by the re-voicing, the calendar repair and the numeric repairs, and then a second time by the review repair, which moved one speech figure in Chapter 516, struck a false claim about a man's speech in Chapter 511, struck a false claim about a question in Chapter 520, cut two sentences of narrator meta-commentary out of Chapters 518 and 519, gave the Sunday in Chapter 518 a reason and a price and corrected the dateline of Chapter 520 that contradicted it, and changed no plot, no date, no name and no money column. The repair is `state/continuity.md` 1262 to 1267 and `state/open-threads.md` 249 to 253. No chapter of Volumes 01 to 10 was edited.**

## Volume 11, Batch 0003 — Chapters 521 to 530, "What the Oldest Protocol Was For"

**Days 1489 to 1542, the fourteenth of Wolfmonth YR 319 to the seventh of Hearthmonth YR 319, a Saturday to a Wednesday, fifty-four days inclusive, five market mornings. Every date in the block is YR 319 and the year turns on day 1596, which is Block 4 and not this one. No chapter of this block falls on a Sunday. Block 0003 is 20,672 words in ten chapters, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included and recounted after the last of five standing repairs to three of the ten files, the sixth repair having been a misattribution of a speaker at `chapter-0530.md` and reverted; the manuscript is 1,546,032 in five hundred and thirty chapters, which is 1,483,501 for Volumes 01 to 10 plus 62,531 for Chapters 501 to 530. The block's argument in one sentence: *the oldest thing in the works is a refusal, and a man in a hand changed the refusal into a selection about a hundred and forty years ago, and the shape the refusal sat on has been going out into nine counties for about a hundred and eighty years with a stroke through the line the refusal was on, and the stroke is the only part of that paper anybody can still be got to swear to.* The word that names the founding paper is printed once in the block, out loud, in a room, by a person, in Chapter 523, and the chapter says on the page that it is printed once.**

**Ch 521 · the fourteenth of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · a hired room over a saddler's · a copy of a copy, and nine words in a margin.** A man of about sixty-three who keeps a lock-up of waste paper puts a sheet on the table and will not take it away again. It is shop-wrapping paper, folded twice, with the same four heads cut into it as the form on the table and **a fifth line ruled under them with no head over it, which this month's form has never had.** A four-figure number cut into the head reads 1840 and nobody in the room can turn it into a year. **The hand on it is not any hand in nine counties today and the woman of about forty-four who has been copying for about nineteen years proves it by getting nine people in the room to write the word *witness* and comparing the downstroke.** A second copy carries nine words in a margin that nobody had read out loud: *Leave the last line blank until you are told*, and the chapter says on the page that nobody may say whose they are and the room declines to spend an hour on it. **Finding: the two copies and the form on the table this month are the same shape, the shape has been going out into nine counties for about a hundred and eighty years, and the difference is not the heads, it is a line.** Nobody named a keeper and nothing was conducted. No speech-length figure is printed. Column 99 pence, the man of about sixty-three's first, on the back of a receipt for a chest of waste paper.

**Ch 522 · the eighteenth of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Wednesday and not a market day · the same room · a line with a stroke through it, and a stroke through a line is not a line.** The woman of about forty-four lays the two sheets two inches apart and says one word. The fifth line is ruled and there is a single stroke through it, in a different ink from the rule, going past the rule at both ends. **Finding: a form that goes out with a struck-out line on it has told somebody something, and what it told them is that somebody was asked and somebody said no, and a line that has been struck out is still a line.** A man of about fifty-three with a barrow says the thing nobody else in the room can say: if you fill that line in, whatever you put in it is a thing a man who was dead a hundred and eighty years ago said no to, and there is nobody here to be asked whether he would have minded. Nobody filled the struck-out line and nobody can say anybody has. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet comes in at about the seventh hour and says two things and the room asks him one question between them, and both of the two are about whoever made the stroke and neither of them is about the stroke** — a repaired paragraph, and the repair is recorded in the batch summary. No speech-length figure is printed. Column 92 pence, the woman of about forty-four's first, on the back of a leaf with no head over it.

**Ch 523 · the twenty-first of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · the same room · the block's event, and the word is printed once.** A woman of about sixty-three who has been in nine counties asking about forms since the third year she kept an inn brings a leaf in a flat box; it is not a form, it has no heads and no lines, and the man of about fifty-eight reads it twice and pushes it across the table and the woman of about forty-four reads it out: *Nothing shuts but two who did not propose it*, nine words. **The man of about fifty-eight says it is a refusal and that a refusal is the oldest thing anybody in nine counties can put a date to, and the woman of about thirty-four makes him decide, and he says the nine words again and then says the one word, out loud, in that room, and the chapter says on the page that it is printed once.** About four of the nine people in that room think it is a rule; about five think it is a sentence somebody liked the sound of, and the two sets do not overlap and the room did not vote. The copyist of about twenty-six and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet both act afterwards, in different ways, and neither is thanked. No speech-length figure is printed; the turn count of the nine words is three and it is printed on the page. Column 111 pence, the man of about fifty-eight's second in this room.

**Ch 524 · the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Wednesday and not a market day · the same room · a strip of paper about two inches wide, and nobody can say who cut it.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet tells the room before the room asks that he took a strip off the front of the second copy with a knife on the Saturday after the word was said, without asking anybody. On the strip, in a hand of about a hundred and forty years ago, is a second nine-word sentence: *A thing once asked is a thing twice agreed*, and it is shorter than the refusal and it is the thing that has been going out into nine counties ever since. **Nobody in the room can say who made the second cut; a county is not a man; the copyist of about twenty-six says out loud that the man is dead and was not a keeper and is not a villain and had about nine years in a county office, and the four things are written on a bare leaf and are not a finding.** **A selection is a refusal with the two witnesses taken out, and a thing with two witnesses taken out is a thing one person can do alone on a Tuesday.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column 117 pence, the man of about thirty-four with a sheet's eleventh in about nine years.

**Ch 525 · the twenty-eighth of Wolfmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · the same room · the coast, reported and not present, and a question nobody asked.** Two things come up the made road from a coast four days off in one fortnight. The first is the fourth return. The second is not a form: a sheet with four heads cut with a knife, a word in the fourth head, and four lines underneath that are a question — *who is in the room when you answer? Nobody asked us. We are asking you.* **The room does not answer it, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet says the reason is not that the question is bad, and the question goes up the made road on the Monday under a return through about nine hands in a saddler's shop.** The protagonist reads the four lines and says the only sentence anybody has sent up that road that says what a room is for was written by somebody who has never been in a room. **No month name and no weekday is spoken by anybody from that sand, and nobody on that coast has said a month or a day in about nine years, and the twenty-second notch is not cut and the water has not been off that mark in twenty-two years and nobody has been sent there.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column 125 pence, the clerk of about twenty-two's second, on the back of a leaf that is not one of the nine.

**Ch 526 · the third of Frostmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · a made lane about a mile and a half · two words have become three, and the third is the word on the return.** The clerk of about thirty-four walks the lane a second time with two sheets, one with the two words from Embermonth and one that is a copy out of a bundle in a saddler's shop. The woman of about twenty-seven at the eleventh of eleven houses says the form asks for one and there is one. **The third word comes out of the fourth house, where a man of about seventy is up, and it is the Chapel Way, and the copy in his coat carries it under the first head in a hand that is not the man in the cart's, and the third head on that copy is under the second one because the sheet it was copied from had a crease in it.** Nobody was asked which of the three is true and nothing was scheduled about choosing. **A form does not ask a place its name; it asks a person for the name the person has, and a place that has three names has three people.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column 137 pence, the woman of about sixty-three's first, on the back of a bill for a yard of straw.

**Ch 527 · the eighth of Frostmonth, YR 319, a Tuesday and not a market day · the same room · the only question anybody asks out loud in ten days, and the room does not answer it.** A man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asks, in front of the other eight, whether a rule is still a rule when one man changed it in a hand. The man of about fifty-eight offers two sentences that are ways of not answering and stops. Nobody answers it, and the question is not answered in prose. **The copyist of about twenty-six says one sentence about it, and it is not about the fourth head, and it is the last thing she says in that run of days: that a line is not a rule and not a finding and is a thing a woman with a knife and a straightedge and no head over her table draws.** Nobody is thanked and nobody is a villain. Seven people in that room each said one thing they had not said before, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote all seven on a bare leaf with no head over it at about the eighth hour, the seventh of them being his own, and he says a leaf with seven things on it and no head over it is going to be the most useful object in that room and that nobody is to be allowed to say so. **None of the seven agreed with any of the other six and the six did not hear themselves described on the leaf and were not asked whether it was right.** No speech-length figure is printed; the two-word utterance is footed on the page. Column 145 pence, the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel's eleventh in about nine years.

**Ch 528 · the sixteenth of Frostmonth, YR 319, a Wednesday and not a market day · the same room · the protagonist, and the one thing in ten days that costs him something.** He is asked nothing about a form and nothing about a rule and nothing about the fourth head, and the two questions he is asked that day are both asked out loud in front of the other eight: the first by the man of about fifty-three with a barrow, which he answers with one word, and the second by the man of about thirty-four with a sheet at about the seventh hour, which is about the eighth sentence and which he answers in one sentence. **He is given no count of his own speech anywhere in the chapter; an earlier printing said he spoke twice and the page contradicts it at five further occasions, and the paragraph now claims only the two answers.** **The dateline carries four hours and not three, because the body stages events at the second, the sixth, the eighth and the eleventh.** **He says nine sentences and they are printed as nine sentences in ten quoted spans, the ninth being two spans of one turn: that the man with the sheet has been up and down that lane more than anybody in the room has been to the rail; that he has a form and a satchel and has asked for nothing for himself; that he was asked a question in Fallowmonth and was not answered; that everything this room has written down for four months is about a piece of paper; that he made one six years ago and held a thing for about a day and a night; that about nine people on the other side of it were never told; that the record being made here is a record of that; that he said no; and that he has not said it in a room before and is not going to be asked to say it in a room again. The one-sentence answer about the eighth sentence is printed after the nine and is not one of them, and the page now says *he went on in the same turn* so that the count foots without inference.** About four of the nine believe him and about five do not and **he does not correct anybody.** Then he asks the man of about fifty-eight, out loud and in front of the other eight, to write his own name beside his own in the back of a book of carts, on the ground that a book with one name in it is a record of a private man's failures and a book with two is a record anybody can be shown. **The man of about fifty-eight writes it and does not read it out, the second time.** He conducts nothing, gets nothing back, overrules nobody and is the reason none of it works. Nothing is reconciled and the difference of three is not found. One printed figure, a turn count of nine sentences, footed on the page. Column 151 pence, the man of thirty-two's eighth, and the one printed figure in the chapter is the count of nine sentences, which foots on the page at nine sentences in ten quoted spans and was recounted after the last prose edit.
