**Ch 529 · the twenty-fourth of Frostmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · the coast's own paper beside a Crown form, and two documents in two counties.** A stone a foot and a half long, with four heads cut into it with a knife and a word in the fourth, comes off the flat stone at the landward end of a coast four days off and is put on the table by the door beside a Crown form. **That is the first time in four months those two documents have been in one room, and the chapter says on the page that they are two documents in two counties, that one of them is a foot and a half long on a flat stone and the other is a sheet off a press in a market town, and that nobody in the room has the right to say which one is older.** **The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel says three things and the three are the three the chapter prints, and the paragraph that used to report them instead of printing them is repaired; Aren Kest's one sentence is now said and is no longer reported in narration.** The woman of about forty-four finds that the four words are the same four words, that the fifth line, where there is one, is about a sixteenth of an inch narrower on the stone, and that the two are two castings off one thing that is not in this county. **The twenty-second notch is not cut and the wall is not on this page, and the woman of about forty-four is the only person in nine counties who has put a hand flat on the stone — a man carried it off that coast and put it down on a table and did not pick it up again, and the woman on that sand who wrapped the parcel in oiled paper does not know there is a room.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column 158 pence, the man of about thirty-four with a sheet's twelfth in about nine years.

**Ch 530 · the seventh of Hearthmonth, YR 319, a Wednesday and not a market day · the same room · the block's end, and it is a decision and not a resolution.** The room takes five hours to go over what it has and does not put one figure of people against another. **The man of about fifty-eight states the finding: a rule was changed by one man in a hand, nobody in nine counties knows who, and nobody is going to find out. He decides to write down what the room found and not to write down who, in his own hand, with a day and an hour against it, and to rule a head over that line, which is the first head he has ruled in that room in eleven years.** About four of the nine think writing it down is right; about five think it is a way of not doing the other thing, and the room did not vote. The man of about fifty-one in a grey coat says the cost out loud and is not thanked. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet says *A county is not a man* and asks for it to be minuted as the fourth time that sentence has been said in that room, and he is right, and the chapter prints that he has said it once before, in Embermonth, and that the woman of about thirty-four has said it twice and is not the same man and is not in the wrong, and the four are the woman in Harvestmonth at `chapter-0505.md`, the man in Embermonth at `chapter-0514.md`, the woman in Wolfmonth at `chapter-0524.md` and the man here; the earlier printing of this entry said he had said it twice before, in Embermonth and in Wolfmonth, and that was wrong, because the Wolfmonth occasion was the woman's and `chapter-0524.md` says so in its own next clause, and the chapter has been restored to *once before, in Embermonth*.** **The hearing is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed. Three documents are on that table and none of them is the form: the Crown form's fourth head is empty, one woman's word is in a fourth head on a returned form, and a struck-out line is on a copy of a copy.** The last clause on the page is that the hearing stands, and the page does not close on a recap and does not close on the tide. No speech-length figure is printed. Column 165 pence, **the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key's second**, the first being the column she kept in Wolfmonth in Chapter 517, and this one on the back of a bill for a lock.

**The ten chapters a Volume 11 writer most often needs.** **Chapter 521** holds the copy of a copy, the fifth line, the nine words in the margin, and the finding that the difference is a line. **Chapter 523** holds the refusal read out loud and the word printed once. **Chapter 524** holds the selection, the four things that are not a finding, and the finding that a selection is a refusal with the two witnesses taken out. **Chapter 525** holds the four lines with a question nobody asked and the bundle that carries them. **Chapter 526** holds the third name for one lane and the sentence about a form asking a person. **Chapter 528** holds the nine sentences and the second name in the back of a book of carts. **Chapter 530** holds the decision, the head cut into the wood over it, the three documents, and the hearing standing.

**What this block changed after the first printing, and it is five things and no plot.** Two money columns were re-ordered and re-worded and none of the ten totals or prices moved. The refusal on the paper in the flat box was recast from fourteen words to nine to match the figure the page had claimed. A claim that a man of thirty-two with a limp said one thing in a room where he said two was cut. A paragraph that said the coast was on no page was cut. A claim that a man of about thirty-four with a sheet had said *a county is not a man* twice in that room was cut, because it is the woman of about thirty-four who has said it there and he had not said it at all. No date moved, no total moved, no name was spent, the fourth head is still empty, the hearing stands, and no chapter of Volumes 01 to 10 was edited.

**The repair pass over this block, which is eight findings and no plot, and it is recorded here because every one of them is a claim about a person or a figure checked against another figure and not against the prose.** Chapter 527's closing paragraph reported a conversation the chapter had already printed and four of its seven claims about what seven people said were false on the page; it now prints what the man of about thirty-four with a sheet did with the leaf instead. Chapter 521 said nobody in that room had asked the protagonist for anything in four months, four days after he was asked a question there and answered it. Chapter 528's dateline named three hours and the body staged four, and a sentence in the same chapter said one question had been asked of him on a day the page prints him asked two. Chapter 529 said one woman was the only person in nine counties who had handled the stone, on a page that prints a man carrying it and a woman wrapping it. Chapter 530's inventory put three bare leaves in Hearthmonth when the last was made in Frostmonth, and its cost column called itself the first its owner had ever kept when Chapter 517 prints her first. Chapter 524 said a sentence had been said twice in that room and Chapter 530 said once, and Chapters 505 and 514 make it three times, which is what both now say. **The two speech-length figures the repair introduced into Chapter 528 were counted off the printed speech, were wrong twice, and were cut rather than corrected. No date moved, no money total moved, no name was spent, the fourth head is still empty, the twenty-second notch is still uncut, the hearing stands, and no chapter of Volumes 01 to 10 was edited.**

## Volume 11, Batch 0004 — Chapters 531 to 540, "The Complete Record"

**Days 1543 to 1596, the tenth of Hearthmonth YR 319 to the first of Thawmonth YR 320, a Saturday to a Monday, fifty-four days inclusive, seven market mornings, and the year turns on the last day of the block and on no other. No chapter of this block falls on a Sunday, so the Sunday rule and the floor that has not been washed are untouched. Batch 0004 is 17,766 words in ten chapters, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, and the manuscript is 1,563,866 in five hundred and forty chapters. **Every figure in this section was recounted on the ten chapter files after the repair pass and none of it is carried forward from the first printing.** The block's argument in one sentence: *a record that includes the man who made the mistake is the only kind anybody can check, and about four hundred people who heard it read out decided that the man who included himself is the reason the thing is believable, and he said no when he was asked whether he was going to correct one of them, and the block does not choose between the two readings and does not have to.* One name was spent, on the reader, and the word that names the founding paper is not printed in this block. No speech-length figure is printed anywhere in the block.**

**Ch 531 · the tenth of Hearthmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · a hired room over a saddler's · a set of sheets with no name, and a head ruled for the first time in eleven years.** A man of thirty-two with a limp brings a packet of sheets and says he has not got a name for it. The man of about fifty-eight asks what it is to be called and the room goes looking out loud, one person at a time, and finds that a day-book has heads for a day, an hour, a person and a thing, that parish rolls have heads for a parish and a year, that a toll-house roll has a head for a gate, and that **there is no head in nine counties for a record of a man's own wrongdoing.** The man of about fifty-three with a barrow gives the reason: a man's own wrongdoing is a thing a man keeps to himself or a thing a court keeps against him, and the first has no head because nobody wants to look at it and the second has a name over it that is not his. **The man of about fifty-eight rules one, the second head he has ruled in that room in eleven years, and the copyist of about twenty-six cuts it with her knife and her straightedge and rules nothing under it and says she will take the knife out of it if anybody rules a line under it in her absence.** Aren Kest writes his own name in the head in his own hand and it takes him longer than writing a name takes a man with a good hand. **Finding: a record with a head over it can be shown to a person and a record without one can only be produced by the man who made it, and that is the whole of what the head is for; it is not there to order anybody, it is not there to be filled in, it is not a fourth head, and there is nothing under it to put an answer in.** The four-and-five device stands once and the man of about fifty-one in a grey coat says both halves out loud. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **101 pence**, the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key's **third**, on the back of nothing in particular. The first printing of this column totalled 88, which is a spent total belonging to `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0449.md`, and it was cleared to 101 by adding pence to two items and not by moving the number.

**Ch 532 · the fourteenth of Hearthmonth, YR 319, a Wednesday and not a market day · the same room · the first page, and it is not the city.** He says that out loud before he turns the sheet round. **The first page is the command in the plainest words he can find: twenty-one, a captain by brevet, the nineteenth of Mudmonth six years ago and a little at about the second hour of the night, a courier and a paper at a gate, a crossing and a district and forty men, a term of about a day and a night, no purpose given, no question asked, and about nine people let over and not sent back, and the plainest words on the page are *I did not send them back*.** The woman of about forty-four reads the page back in the voice she uses for a form and catches *about nine* and he says he counted nine and may have lost two behind a buttress. Nobody in the room argues with it and nobody in the room is thanked for reading it. **The record does not say where the fever came from and will not, and the reason is on the page: a record that ends in a cause is a record that has found somebody, and the somebody is him.** The man of about fifty-eight gives the woman of about thirty-four's question one sentence: a head with a name over it is a thing a clerk is given to fill in, and in about a year it will be a return, and a return goes out. No speech-length figure is printed; a nine-word figure that stood here in the first printing was an eleven-word sentence and was cut. Column **114 pence**, the man of about thirty-four with a sheet's **thirteenth** in about nine years.

**Ch 533 · the seventeenth of Hearthmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · the same room · a word, and the die, and a printed line that is not a ruled line.** The second page is read out by the man of about thirty-four with a sheet and Aren Kest corrects him once, in one word. **The account came up the made road on a cart with the forms and he signed for the cart, and the cause the account gives is the word *none* — and *none* is printed at the head of every form in nine counties, in letters smaller than the four heads, cut in the die with them, and it reads *Fever in this parish: none since the last return*.** The man of about fifty-eight says out loud that a printed line is not a ruled line, that a printed line is not a fifth line, and that a fifth line is not a fourth head, before anybody in the room has called it anything. **Finding: a man who has recorded the mistake he made with a piece of paper has to go and record the mistake he made with a word, and he cannot do the second one by writing more, because the second one was not a not-writing; the second one was a word he took off a cart, and the word is still going out.** The man of about fifty-three with a barrow puts the form back on the pile face up, which nobody had done in four months, and the man of about fifty-eight lets it stand and rules no head over it. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **103 pence**, the man of about fifty-three with a barrow's **first**. The first printing of this column totalled 98, which is a spent total belonging to `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0471.md`, and it was cleared to 103 by adding pence to one item and not by moving the number.

**Ch 534 · the twenty-second of Hearthmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · one page, and the only one he reads himself.** The third page is a copy of a thing not in this county: a sign made with a pen pressed hard that does not spell anything and is not a hand, and above it a day written out in full in his own hand of about six years ago. **He reads the last four lines of it aloud in a voice about nine people in that room had not heard before, and the four lines are: *My mother made a crossing. It was on the day written above and not on any day I can find out. I was not there. I have not asked her, and I have not said any of this out loud before.*** The question about the date is asked by the man of about thirty-four with a sheet and it is not about a road: *Whose hand wrote the day?* He asks one further thing about where the leaf is kept and the answer is that it is kept apart from the record. **The first printing of this chapter's dateline called this *the only question anybody asked that day* on a page that prints three questions, and the dateline now says that one of the people in the room was not thanked for asking a question that was about a date and not about a road.** The answer is that it is the day he wrote it and not the day she crossed, and that he has not asked her which day it was and is not going to. The copyist of about twenty-six asks the one question that day that is about the sign, and the woman of about forty-four says she could not tell what made it and the record does not say on purpose. **Nothing in the chapter is a crossing, nothing was conducted, nothing was cut, no mechanism is explained, no word about a roadmark is printed, and the page is a piece of paper with a sign on it and a date a man wrote six years ago.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column **126 pence**, the man of about fifty-eight's **third** in that room.

**Ch 535 · the twenty-ninth of Hearthmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · an upper room over a tallow chandler's, hired for the day · the reading.** About four hundred people are in it and **the office is not in the room**: the man of about fifty-eight sent word on the Wednesday in nine words, and the nine words are *this record is not what a badge can hold*, and they are footed on the page at nine. **A woman of forty-one called Hester Ollett reads it out, all of it, and the last of it she reads slowly.** She asked what she was paid before she said yes and was paid and nobody thanked her, **and what she was paid is not in her column and she has never said whether it should be.** **About four hundred people who came to hear a thing about a form were told a thing about a man, and it was not a shock, it was a change of subject.** Nobody filled anything in; there was no form, no clerk, no press, no die and no badge in the room. At about the fifth hour a man on the second step said, to nobody, *where is he*, and the answer came back out of the floor and it was a place and it was wrong. **Aren Kest was in the street outside that door from about the fourth hour to the seventh and about four hundred people went past him and he let them all go by.** The fourth page is a list of five things he does not know and the first of them is how many were behind the second buttress. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **146 pence**, Hester Ollett's **first**, and the only name this block spends, and one of its three four-hundreds is the coast's nine hundred rather than the sand's, which has a different figure on it.

**Ch 536 · the fourth of Goatmonth, YR 319, a Tuesday and not a market day · the same room · the cost of the reading, and an answer that is wrong.** The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asks the question he asked in Frostmonth, and this time a woman answers it: that a rule a man can put his own name under is a better rule than one nobody can be shown. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet says why she is wrong in three sentences and is right, and about four of the nine agree with him and about five do not, the two sets do not overlap, and nobody agrees with either of them out loud.** The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel does not argue with her and says he will carry it down the road instead, and says he will tell them what was said in the order it was said in. **The four-and-five device stands once and the man of about fifty-three with a barrow does something because of it: he has the first page written out on a bare leaf in the man of about thirty-four's own hand, folds it into four, and says he is going to read one page out loud at a rail of a common on the Thursday and that nobody is to come with him.** Aren Kest comes in at about the seventh hour and stands at the end of the table and is asked nothing. **The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel does not argue with either of them, and when he carries it down the road he says he will tell them what was said in the order it was said in and not which of the two was right, because a rule can be checked is a true thing and a witness is not a rule is a true thing and he has worked out that the two of them are each right about one half of it and neither is right about the other's half.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column **150 pence**, the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel's **twelfth** in about nine years. **The first printing called it the thirteenth, and the last column he keeps before this one is the eleventh in Chapter 527, so there is no twelfth anywhere between them and the chapter now says twelfth.**

**Ch 537 · the eighth of Goatmonth, YR 319, a Saturday and a market day · a made lane about a mile and a half · a name, and four hundred copies that go on being copies.** A clerk of about thirty-four walks the lane with nothing in his hand, back up it to the fourth house and on down it again to the chapel, which is what the lane makes him do. A man with a barrow read a page out loud at the rail of a common on the Thursday to about four people who live at the end of that lane, and **what arrived at the eleventh of eleven houses was a man's name**, said correctly and out loud by a woman who keeps a goat and who did not know till she said it that she had it. **Finding: a record read out loud in a market town does not get to a made lane; a lane learns about a thing the way a lane learns about a form, from a person who came back, and by the time the sheet would have got there the person has been there and gone.** The woman of about twenty-seven says that it has moved from paper to people and asks what it is for, and nobody in that lane can tell her, and she is not thanked and nobody agrees with her out loud. The man of about seventy in the fourth house says it is nothing to do with them. ****The copies went on being copies, and the mechanism that makes them is that a man in a saddler's shop has put the top sheet of every bundle that has come through that shop since Harvestmonth on his board and handed the rest down the lane as people asked for it, and no rate is printed because no rate would survive the arithmetic of about four hundred kitchens in four months.** About nine people have compared theirs and the nine do not agree about a place, one carries the Chapel Way under its first head with its third head under its second one, and nobody regulates copying and nobody is thanked for it and the three names for one lane are not resolved.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column **163 pence**, the woman of about sixty-three's **second**.

**Ch 538 · the thirteenth of Goatmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room and the street outside it · a panic, and the finding that a room produces one on its own.** A man of about fifty-one in a grey coat has been out into the market twice and comes back with a version of a story that nobody in that room put there. **Nobody started it: the story is that a record says where four roads are, and there are no four roads in the record and none in that room and nobody in that room has ever been able to say how many there are; the four comes from the four heads, counted by a person in a hurry.** A man of about thirty-eight with a satchel says the people in the street are right about the one thing that matters, which is that a road anybody can find out about can be shut. A person came up the lane with a reason and asked whether the room was going to shut the roads and the man of about fifty-eight said no and was then asked what the room is for and did not answer, and the reason is on the page: a room that answers a street has been turned into a street. The four-and-five device stands once. **Nobody was sent to find out, no order was made, no head was filled in on any form, and the page says that a plan is a room, two clerks, a chairman with a chain of office that is a badge, a form with four heads and the fourth of them empty, and a panic — and no chapter says whose any of it is.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column **167 pence**, Gault Prentice's **third**. **The first printing called it the first he has ever kept, and Chapters 503 and 513 print a first and a second, so the chapter now says third.**

**Ch 539 · the twentieth of Goatmonth, YR 319, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · the one word, and the block's cost in one line.** The story has been going round since the twenty-ninth of Hearthmonth, and **the ones who came and said he is the reason the rest of it stands up are right, and the ones who said he is the reason anybody should have believed any of it in the first place are a different set, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote both on one leaf and ruled nothing over them and refused to count either.** He asks one question and it is not about a form: *Are you going to correct them?* **The answer is *No*, and that is the whole of it, and he does not say why and the room does not ask, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote two words on a bare leaf and turned it face down and has never told anybody in nine counties what they are.** The cost of the block is on the page in one line: he made himself findable on a Saturday in Hearthmonth and about four hundred people have found him, and being found is the thing he bought, and he did not know it would be this. No paragraph accounts for his silence. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **175 pence**, the man of thirty-two's **ninth** since the twenty-fifth day of a month carried at thirty in the year before this one.

**Ch 540 · the first of Thawmonth, YR 320, a Monday and not a market day · the same room · the year turns and the block does not resolve.** The clerk of about twenty-two writes the day at the top of a fresh page and does not write the year anywhere on it, and about five people are in the room at the first hour. Aren Kest has not been in it for eleven days and nobody in that room said anything about where he was, and **the clerk of about twenty-two is one of those five and has been since the first hour, which is what his column belongs to, and the man of about fifty-eight says the standing aloud to about five people instead of about nine and the difference is four.** **The record is in public and true and complete; the hearing stands and is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed and the man of about fifty-eight says it out loud on the first day of every month; the fourth head of the Crown's form is empty and the word in a fourth head on the coast's own sheet is on a different document in a different county and the two have not been in one room since the twenty-fourth of Frostmonth; the three words for one lane are still three and the room has adopted none and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote down what the woman of about twenty-seven said at the eleventh of those eleven houses and read none of it back to anybody; a shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled and nobody was sent; and a coast four days off has a woman of about fifty-one at a post with twenty-two notches cut in a piece of driftwood and the twenty-second is not cut and the notches are years and not tides and nobody has been sent to that coast.** The page does not close on a recap and does not close on a person agreeing: the last clause is that the year was Thawmonth and it was the first and nobody in that room wrote it down. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **182 pence**, the clerk of about twenty-two's **third**.

**The ten chapters a Volume 11 writer most often needs.** **Chapter 531** holds the head, the ruling of it, the reason there was none, and the finding about what a head is for. **Chapter 532** holds the command in the plainest words and the refusal to end the record in a cause. **Chapter 533** holds the word *none*, the printed line at the head of the form, and the finding that a mistake made with a word cannot be recorded by writing more. **Chapter 534** holds the crossing his mother made, the sign, the day he wrote, and the refusal to say what the sign is. **Chapter 535** holds the reading, the reader, the office not being in the room, and about four hundred people told a thing about a man. **Chapter 536** holds the wrong answer and the reason it is wrong. **Chapter 537** holds the name arriving at a lane and the finding that a lane learns from a person. **Chapter 538** holds the panic, the four heads becoming four roads, and the page's own statement of what a plan is. **Chapter 539** holds the one-word answer and the block's cost in one line. **Chapter 540** holds the year, the hearing standing, the fourth head empty, and the twenty-second notch uncut.

**What this block's own hand found after its first printing and again in a second pass, in twenty-two findings and no plot, and it is recorded here because every one of them is a claim about what a person said, did, was asked or believed, or a figure checked against something other than the prose it described, and not one of the twenty-two was visible to a sweep.** Chapter 532 called an eleven-word sentence nine words and the figure was cut. Chapter 535 called an eighteen-word sentence nine words and the sentence was recast to nine and re-counted. Chapter 534 said the man of about thirty-four with a sheet had read both of the first two pages aloud on a page that prints the first read by the woman of about forty-four. Chapter 533 said a man had had eleven years of looking at a crossing on a page whose command is six years ago and a little. Chapter 536 said a speech was four sentences when the page prints three, said a man had been down nine counties in six weeks when the page's own dates make it nearer a fortnight, and claimed nine sentences that are not printed. Chapter 537 said the clerk of about thirty-four had come down the lane on the Thursday and then said he had not. Chapter 538 gave two intervals that the page's own dates contradict. Chapter 540 said eleven days as nine. **Eight more were found in the verification pass this phase ran over the block: Chapter 534's dateline and Chapter 534's body each claimed a single question on a page that prints three; Chapter 537 said the man of about thirty-four had been asked nothing by anybody on a page that prints him asking the woman at the rail; Chapter 538 counted its own two-sets occasions as the fourth since the tenth of Hearthmonth and now says it is not the first; Chapter 540 said nine people had not been on a page in that room since the twenty-fifth of Harvestmonth and now says none of them has ever come into that room; Chapter 535 named five counts of persons and then called them four; Chapters 535, 536 and 539 each paired the wrong two counts as the same figure and not the same figure, and the right pair is the lane's four hundred with the four hundred that went under a second head in Harvestmonth; Chapter 536's column ordinal was one too high and Chapter 538's was two too low; Chapter 538 said a fortnight was last week.** **And two of them were duplication, which is the class that would have shipped: the block's own first certification claimed a longest shared run of 8 words against the five hundred and thirty prior chapters and 8 words inside the block, and the true figures on the declared basis are 20 and 20, and before the repair there was a 44-word run between the money columns of Chapters 534 and 539 and a 35-word run between Chapter 537's dateline and Chapter 526's.** **A third pass found eight more and they are at `state/continuity.md` 1293, and the one it did not repair is the block's dating, which is recorded there and referred rather than silently changed.** Two money totals collided with spent totals and were cleared by adding pence to the column and not by moving the number. Two claimed figures of persons were cut because a person in the scene could have counted them. No date moved after the calendar check, no name but one was spent, the fourth head is still empty, the twenty-second notch is still uncut, the hearing stands, and no chapter of Volumes 01 to 10 was edited.

## Volume 11, Batch 0005 — Chapters 541 to 550, "What the Court Cannot Do"

**Days 1597 to 1650, the second of Thawmonth YR 320 to the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 320, a Tuesday to a Saturday, fifty-four days inclusive, eight market mornings, and the year turned on day 1596, which was the last day of the block before this one. No chapter of this block falls on a Sunday, so the Sunday rule and the floor of the room that has not been washed since the eighth of Wolfmonth are untouched and the floor was not washed by anybody. Batch 0005 is 25,053 words in ten chapters, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, and the manuscript is 1,588,897 in five hundred and fifty chapters; the block was 25,128 and the manuscript 1,588,994 before the Batch 0005 review repair, and the close took neither figure, and the two repairs the review forced afterwards took five words out of `chapter-0547.md` and none out of `chapter-0506.md`. Every figure in this section was recounted on the ten chapter files after the last prose edit of the repair and none of it is carried forward from a first printing. The block's argument in one sentence: *a room of about nine people can do one thing, which is to record that an order may not act on a road without that road's own answer to a question put to it in its own words, and it cannot put a city back and does not pretend to, and the record that made the finding checkable is the one that put a man's name on a page.* No name was spent by this block, the word that names the founding paper is not printed in it, and no speech-length figure is printed anywhere in it.**

**Ch 541 · the second of Thawmonth, YR 320, a Tuesday and not a market day · a hired room over a saddler's · the finding, and the four reasons it cannot be done.** The man of about fifty-eight reads the finding off a leaf, puts the leaf face down, and says the whole of what the room can do in about four minutes: *an order may not act on a road without that road's own answer to a question put to it in its own words*, and then, in the same breath, that a rule about procedure is not a promise about what anybody will do, because in about four years somebody will read the first sentence on its own and it will sound like a promise, and the reason it will is that it is the shape of a promise, **and a shape is the only part of a rule that travels.** It is on a bare leaf with no head over it and it is not in the room's book, and the reason is on the page. Then the man of about thirty-four with a sheet gives the four failures in the order a man walks a road in: there is no head in nine counties for a question; there is no head in nine counties for an order, and the word order is on no paper in that room; **a road cannot be asked a question in its own words, because what a person will tell you is what somebody told him, and the somebody is in that room or in a house in that town;** and not one of nine road offices has a procedure for it, and eight said some version of *it is the form we have always used* and one wrote it down and dated it and it is in the satchel. The man of about fifty-three with a barrow says that about four of them think the sentence is a rule and about five think it is a man finding a room where he does not have to go and find out, and asks whether it can be read out loud, and nobody tells him he can and nobody tells him he cannot. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet says it is worth more than the whole of what anybody in that room came for, and the room does not vote, and a vote would be a finding and there has not been one in five months.** Aren Kest is asked nothing at all. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **113 pence**, the man of about fifty-eight's **fourth** in that room; the first printing totalled 112, which `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0415.md` already owns, and it was cleared by adding a penny to one item and not by moving the number.

**Ch 542 · the eighth of Thawmonth, YR 320, a Monday and not a market day · one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane about two hundred miles up a made road from a coast · the two of them in a room, and the first distributed thing anybody in nine counties has built.** They are in that room for about seven hours together for the first time since a Tuesday in Rainmonth in the year before this one, a woman of about thirty-one with a pen is in it for all of it, **and neither of the two says one word about the four and a half years, and the narration says so once and does not say it again.** The room has a press standing open with no date, a register at seven questions and no findings, a day-book at nine entries of a thing nobody asked, a drawer about eleven inches deep that is not open, and an outgoing sheet that has carried a fourth ruled line with no head over it for four and a half years. Nine leaves go out with the same four heads ruled in the same hand, three filled, the fourth ruled and empty, and a question written under the fourth head by a person and not by an office, in the same words on all nine. **The challenge to the office is not a challenge to any person and is not a refusal: a question under a fourth head on a leaf is the only one of about nine documents in that county that can go out and come back with something in it that nobody in an office chose, and the reason it is the only one is that every other one asks a person to fill a head, and a filled head is a thing an office can read and a question is a thing an office can only forward, and forwarding it is what an office is for.** Nobody has to be sent, because a man who goes down a made road with a sheet in his coat is a man going down a made road and a man who is sent is a man somebody sent. He cuts the ninth leaf in half and gives one half to the keeper of that room and keeps the other, so that neither of them has all of them, and she says that is not a reason and he says it is a precaution, and neither of them says one word about it again. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **121 pence**, the woman of about twenty-nine's **ninth** in four and a half years.

**Ch 543 · the eleventh of Thawmonth, YR 320, a Thursday and a market day · the same room over the saddler's · the two sets of four ruled heads, and the answer to the offer that has been on the table since Block 0002.** There are two sets of four ruled heads in nine counties and nobody outside those two rooms knows the other one exists; neither is a copy of a return, neither is an institution, and neither has a name, a keeper, a register or a day. **The second set's fourth head is about a sixteenth of an inch narrower than the three above it and the first set's is not, and that is the whole of the difference, and the reason was given once in that room to about four people and to nobody else, and the copyist says on the page that she has not told the room why and is not going to.** The offer is answered and the answer is worse than the letter it came in: the four names go up the made road to nine counties in a sheet of oiled paper with nothing on the outside of it, so that a road may complain to a person, **and the four names are still off the letter and the letter is in a coat in that room.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet puts the two sets two inches apart and says that if anybody puts a head on either of them, then within a year somebody will be asked to fill it, and a road that does not want to be found will be a road that has been given a form to refuse on. **A prohibition is a line written in a room's book by a man with a badge, and the page may say that a rule about procedure is not a promise about what anybody will do; the man of about thirty-four with a sheet says the headless leaf is the most useful object in that room and forbids anybody to say so.** Nobody agreed with him and nobody argued with him, and the man of about fifty-one in a grey coat said out loud that the answer is worse and he is not going to argue with it and would rather be argued with than not. Aren Kest was in the room from the first hour to the eighth and was asked nothing about a form, the fourth head or the two sets. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **131 pence**, the man of about thirty-four with a sheet's **fourteenth** in about nine years.

**Ch 544 · the eighteenth of Thawmonth, YR 320, a Thursday and a market day · the same room · the third asking of the question, and the answer.** The question is the same question in the same words in three rooms in nine counties: *is a rule still a rule when one man changed it in a hand?* He had said it in Frostmonth and got nothing; he said it again in Goatmonth and a woman answered it and the answer was wrong and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet gave the reason in three sentences and was right. He said it a third time at about the second hour with the satchel between his knees, and then **it is answered at about the fifth hour by the man of about fifty-three with a barrow, who was not asked the question and answered it**: a rule is a rule when one man changed it in a hand, and a rule nobody can name the maker of is a rule anybody can be shown, and he has wanted to be shown a thing for about nine years and nobody has ever shown him one, and the reason he has now got one is not that the man was brave, it is that the man wrote his name on a page. **The answer is allowed now and was not allowed twice before, and the reason is on the page: the man of about fifty-eight says a room of about nine people in a hired room over a saddler's is not a place where a thing about a rule in nine counties can be found, and the answer came from a man with a barrow and not from a man with a badge, and that is the only reason the room writes it down at all.** The man of about fifty-three with a barrow asks for three things and gets all three: write it down, put no head over it, do not read it back. Nobody thanked the man with the barrow for the answer. The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel says it is the first answer the room has got in five months from somebody who is not paid and is not a clerk, and that the smallness was in the room and not in the county, and asks for that to be written down and not said again. **The hearing stands and is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed, and it is said out loud at the ninth hour to about four people instead of about nine.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column **141 pence**, the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key's **fourth** ever; the first printing totalled 136, which is a barred numeral, and the column was corrected by raising an item and not by moving the number.

**Ch 545 · the twentieth of Thawmonth, YR 320, a Saturday and a market day · a made lane about a mile and a half out of the market town, past a common, to eleven houses and a chapel · the edge, and the page.** He walks the whole of the made lane with nothing in his hand and nobody asks him anything, and he stands where the lane stops being made, which is not a gate and not a stile and not a boundary stone and has never been marked. **He does not go on, and nothing happened, and no crossing was made and none was booked.** **What the edge cost him is the page**: a page is a thing with one man's name at the top of it, and a man who is in two rooms at once has a page that is a thing about two men, and a thing about two men is a thing nobody can be shown. He kept the page. He paid it in about four seconds with his stick in his right hand and nobody in that lane saw it and nobody in nine counties was told. The man of about thirty-four was at the rail of the common with a sheet in his hand and no straightedge and neither man spoke. **The woman of about twenty-seven at the eleventh house opens her door about two paces wide and says one thing to the lane and not to him — that the cart road behind the chapel has been under water twice since the snow went and once before that, that it is not on any paper anywhere and nobody has been to see whether a cart goes along it, and that she is not asking him to go and see, because a person who goes and sees is a person somebody sent — and he did not answer her, was not asked to, and did not go and see.** A lane keeps a version of a road the way a room keeps a finding, and the two are not added. He comes back up the mile and a half and goes into the room at about the eighth hour, the room is shut on Sundays and not on a Saturday, and the floor has not been washed since the eighth of Wolfmonth and was not washed that day. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **155 pence**, the man of thirty-two's **tenth** since the twenty-fifth of a month carried at thirty in the year before the year before this one; the first printing called it his eleventh and his ninth stands in Chapter 539.

**Ch 546 · the twenty-seventh of Thawmonth, YR 320, a Saturday and a market day · the same room over the saddler's · two documents, and a word in a fourth head that is not the form's.** A sheet of foolscap with four heads came up a made road, and under the fourth head a person has written one sentence in a hand that is not a press and not a die: **the fourth head is empty here too and has been for four and a half years and nobody has told her why.** The second thing came up on foot in the hands of a woman of about fifty-three who takes in washing, four miles, and it is a returned form from a county two hundred miles off with three heads filled and one word in the fourth, and she is the woman who put it there and nobody asked her about it, and the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key says *do not ask her that* before anybody has, which is the fourth time in five months she has said it and she has never said it twice in a day. **She is not thanked, not made a witness, not made a villain, not made a symbol, not made a subject, and her word stays where it is and is going to stay there.** The man of about thirty-one with a pen then gives the one difference nobody in nine counties has ever put on a page: this one has a stroke through a line, that one has a word in a head, **a line somebody has been through is not an empty line and is not a filled one**, and in about four years a clerk will pick that sheet up and see a line with a stroke through it and it will be true that somebody wrote no and it will not be true that anybody asked, and one of those two ends has a man's name on it and the other has nothing on it at all. The copyist says she has ruled four thousand lines and copied about four hundred with a stroke through them and has never once been asked what one meant, and that this morning she was asked, and by a person who takes in washing, and she is not going to decide what that makes the two of them. **Four documents in four counties and none of the four is the Crown's form, and the page says so before anybody in that room can say it.** No speech-length figure is printed. Column **171 pence**, the woman of about fifty-three's **second** ever, and it is kept on a washing board and a washing board is not a thing a room is charged for.

**Ch 547 · the second of Mudmonth, YR 320, a Thursday and a market day · the room over the weigh-house two hundred miles off · four lines from another county, and the road that has been careful with.** Four lines come up the lane in a bundle with a return on top of them, in a hand that is not one person's, written by somebody who put four heads and four lines under them on a piece of paper the size of a return and left it on a flat stone at the landward end of a coast: *You have nine hundred and about four trades and one word. Who is in the room when you answer? Nobody asked us. We are asking you.* **The room does not answer it, and the reason is not that the question is bad, and the man of about fifty-eight said out loud in a room in Frostmonth that it is a good question, in the voice of a man who is going to have to defend it in about four months.** The woman of about twenty-nine says the same thing she has said every time it has come up a lane: it is addressed to whoever opens it, in nine counties the answer to a question like that is whoever is nearest, she is the nearest, and if she answers it then the answer is a woman in a room two hundred miles off from the sand it was written on, and a woman in a room is a person and a person can be found. Nobody agreed and nobody argued. **Then the woman of about thirty-one with a pen and the woman of about twenty-nine put a figure of nine hundred four lines from a figure of four hundred and nine, and the page says that a leaf which sets one of them down cannot hold the other one four lines away from her, and everybody in that room could see it while it was happening and nobody could do a thing about it, and neither of them has put the two together in four and a half years and neither of them is going to. That paragraph was repaired: its first printing named the defect in the author's words rather than the room's, and no register in this book is allowed to carry the author's words.** The woman of about thirty-one with a pen then says the thing that came out of it: a form does not ask a place its name, it asks a person, the answer it gets is the name the person has, a place with three names has three people, that is what a form is, **and the fault is that nine counties of forms were cut with a head that asks for one and there is no head in any of the nine that asks how many.** Aren Kest, who came up the lane on the Wednesday, gives the finding in about four sentences and says it to the four lines and not to anybody, and nobody writes it down, and he does not say what the sign on the third page of his own record is and nobody asks him. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **208 pence**, the woman of about thirty-one with a pen's **first** ever; the first printing printed 178 over a column whose ten items foot at 208, and the printed total was corrected to the sum and the ten prices were not touched, because the standing rule is that a price is not moved to fit a total.

**Ch 548 · the ninth of Mudmonth, YR 320, a Thursday and a market day · the same room over the saddler's · the finding published, in a fourth line that is not a fourth head.** The copyist rules four lines on a sheet of foolscap with a straightedge and a knife, and nobody ordered it and nobody asked her to, and she says one sentence before she does it: four lines is not four heads, and a head is a cut in a die and a line is a thing a person makes. **The finding goes up a made road on that same morning in the fourth line of that sheet, and the fourth line is a fourth line and not a fourth head, and it is on a different paper from the Crown's form and in a different hand from the Crown's form, and the page says so before anybody in that room can say it, and a head is a shape and not an order.** The fourth line is the finding with a man's name in it, and it is the only one of the four lines with anybody's name on it, and there is no head over it and nothing ruled above or below it. The man of about fifty-eight ruled it himself with a knife he has never used and it showed, and said out loud that a rule that is not written down is not a rule, it is a habit, and four people's habit does not travel out of a hired room on its own. **Nobody in that room said the fourth head ought not to be there and nobody said it ought to be filled in, and the fourth head of the Crown's form is ruled and empty at the second hour and at the ninth and will be empty on the Monday the bundle goes, and no office in nine counties has filled it and no person has.** Then the man of about thirty-four with a sheet does the thing that is the cost of the whole of this volume: he has carried the first page of that record down about nine counties in a satchel since Goatmonth and read it out loud twice at a rail and once in a bar and **he has never once read the man's name, and the reason he has not answered the question he has been asked about four times in about nine years is that a man who carries a page with another man's name at the top of it is a man who can be shown that name, and a name that can be shown is a name that can be said.** The man of about fifty-three with a barrow says *then stop carrying it*, and the answer is no, because the other half is that a road anybody can find out about can be shut, and the only reason anybody in nine counties knows there is a fourth head at all is that about four hundred unregulated copies of a return went down a made road on a cart and every one was made by a person in a kitchen. Nobody thanked him and nobody argued with him. **Nobody in that room voted, and the clerk of about twenty-two laid both hands flat on the open book, and the copyist put the knife down on the sill and did not pick it up again that morning, and the man with the barrow put his coat on over his shirt and said nothing about where he was going and was not asked.** The paragraph that stood here in the first printing was the non-existence of a man of about thirty-three and an account of why the page was arranged as it was, and it is gone. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **186 pence**, the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel's **thirteenth** in about nine years; the first printing called it his fourteenth and his twelfth stands in Chapter 536.

**Ch 549 · the sixteenth of Mudmonth, YR 320, a Thursday and a market day · the same room, and the market below it · a sheet of foolscap that has been carried, and a man who is a public target.** The sheet went into the market on the Thursday, in a bundle with nine returns, and a man in a saddler's shop put it on a board; on the Saturday a woman who sells fish near the weigh-house asked the man who keeps the board what was on it, and he said it was paper, and she read it standing up, and about four people read it standing up on the Saturday, and by the Tuesday about four hundred people in this town of about two thousand had read a piece of foolscap with four lines on it standing up in a street. **On the eighth day the same woman came up the lane at about the fifth hour and asked the man of about fifty-eight one question, and he answered it, and about nine people heard the answer, and about four hundred people in that town can now say where a man of thirty-two with a limp sleeps.** A finding is worth more than the whole of what anybody wanted because a person can carry it and about nine hundred sheets a month cannot be carried by anybody, because they are in a bundle in a cart. **Nobody filled a head, nobody cut a line, the four lines are four lines, and the fourth of them has a man's name in it and it is not a fourth head, and the fourth head of the Crown's form is empty and has been for about five months and nobody in this county has filled it and no office has.** The woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key then asks the one question nobody in that room had asked — *what happens to him?* — and the man of about fifty-eight refuses to answer it, and the reason is on the page: a room that says what happens to a man is a room that has started keeping a book of people, and he has a rule of his own that he does not write anybody's name on a bare leaf, and a name went into a fourth line this month on a sheet of foolscap that he did not rule and he is not going to take it out. **The man of about fifty-one in a grey coat says one thing and it is the only thing he says in that room in about a month: four hundred people have read a piece of paper standing up and about four hundred and nine have not, the four hundred and nine do not know there is a room, there is no head in nine counties for them and there is not going to be one, and that is the thing he has been building towards for about five months without knowing he was building towards it, and he would like it written without a head over it.** Nobody thanked him and he is not a villain and he is not a knave. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **194 pence**, the man of about fifty-one in a grey coat's **fourth** ever; the first printing called it his second, and his second stands in Chapter 513 and his third in Chapter 538.

**Ch 550 · the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth, YR 320, a Saturday and a market day, and the volume's last day · the same room, and a market in the street below it · the finding in the book, and a half leaf nobody turns over.** A man of thirty-two with a limp was not in that room. He had said to nobody that he would be there and nobody had asked him; he came up the lane at about the third hour and there was a market in the street and he was in it and he was not upstairs, and a man of about twenty-nine with a handcart knew him from a street and said the name out loud twice, and he stood in a market and let a market town say his name and did not say it back. **Nobody in that room said anything about where he was.** The finding goes into the room's book on the thirteenth of Mudmonth with a pen and not a knife, and the man of about fifty-eight says out loud that he is not cutting a second head over it in eleven years, that the book has one head cut into the wood over one line and that head was cut for a different thing, that the finding is in the book and is under a head that is not its own, and that those are two separate facts, **and that a clerk in about four years is going to find a sentence under a head that was cut for something else and is going to have to decide on his own what it is about, and that is the correct condition for it.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet says the second half: it has been in that book for twelve days and nobody in nine counties has done one single thing differently because of it, and that is not a complaint about anybody in that room, and the second half is the one that will still be true in about four years. **And the man of about fifty-eight says out loud what the room cannot do, and nobody asked him to and nobody wrote it down: it cannot put a city back, it cannot open anything, it cannot send anybody to find out, it cannot fill the fourth head, and a rule that says a thing may not be done is a different object from a thing being not done.** The man of about fifty-one in a grey coat says the one thing he says in about a month, and it is about the room and not about a road: the difference between the room on the second of a month carried at thirty and this one is a sentence and a sheet of foolscap with four lines on it, and what has happened to it is not that it got bigger, it is that it got a man in it. Then the last thing anybody in that county did with a piece of paper. **Half a leaf came back** — the half the man of thirty-two kept when he cut the ninth leaf in two, carried down a made road by the man of about thirty-four with a sheet in the last week of Thawmonth, who was not sent and was not nobody-sent, and it went to the landward end of a coast four days off and came back up nineteen days or about nine more on the sixth of Mudmonth and was put on that table face down, **and it has been face down for nineteen days and nobody in that room has turned it over, including the man with the badge, the copyist with the knife, the clerk whose job it is to enter things, and the man with a barrow who is the only person there who has ever been on the wrong side of a line.** The woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key says what it is: not a form, not the form, not a return, not on a bundle in nine counties, four heads with a question under the fourth one, the question under the head and not in it, the fourth head ruled and empty, and no office has filled it and nobody is going to. **The hearing stands, and there is no bench in that room and there has never been one and nobody in that county is sentenced to anything by anything said in it.** The Crown's form's fourth head is still empty and will be empty on the Monday the bundle goes. The three words for one lane are still three and the room has not taken one over. A shut road four hundred miles off is shut. The twenty-second notch is not cut. Nothing was opened and nobody was sent. No speech-length figure is printed. Column **226 pence**, the man of about thirty-four with a sheet's **fifteenth** in about nine years.

---

## Volume 11 Close — Chapters 501 to 550, and the volume as a finished book

**The fifty chapters, the five blocks, the closing figures, and what a Volume 12 writer inherits. The full record is `outline/volumes/volume-11-close.md`. Every figure below was counted on the chapter files after the close's last prose edit.**

### The shape of the volume

**A public inquiry in a hired room over a saddler's in a market town of about two thousand people, shut on Sundays, and the whole of its act in fifty chapters is to write down that an order may not act on a road without that road's own answer to a question put to it in its own words, and to say out loud in the same breath that a rule about procedure is not a promise about what anybody will do.** It is a Crown form with four heads cut into it — a place, a figure of people, a trade, and a fourth that is ruled and empty — going out of a room over a weigh-house about nine hundred sheets a month and coming back with three heads filled and the fourth empty, five months running, in nine counties, and one county cut its fourth head off clean with a knife and took a quarter inch of paper with it. **Nobody filled it, nobody ordered it filled, and the volume's answer is that the way you stop a line from being filled in is not to fill it and is not to remove it: it is to have somebody else write on the same paper first.** A man of thirty-two with a limp published a complete record of his own wrongdoing with his own name at the top of it, was read aloud once to about four hundred people by a woman who asked what she was paid before she said yes and was not thanked, became a public target, walked to where a made lane stops being made and did not go on, and **what the edge cost him was a page**. A half leaf went down a made road to a coast four days off and came back and has been face down on a table for nineteen days and nobody has turned it over.

**Days 1381 to 1650, the twenty-sixth of Rainmonth YR 319 to the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 320, 269 days elapsed and 270 inclusive, and the year turns on day 1596, the first of a month, which is the last day of Block 0004.** Thirty-four of the fifty chapters fall on a market morning at 8 / 6 / 5 / 7 / 8 by block, and **two fall on a Sunday, at days 1434 and 1483, which every record before this close said was none.**

### The five blocks and their costs

| | Chs | Days | Words, after the close | `about`/1,000 | The block's cost | The block's gift |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | --- |
| 1 | 501–510 | 57 incl. | **20,919** | 27.30 | the fourth head, empty in nine counties before anybody argued about it, and a woman told twice it is not hers to cut | a return, a room, and a rule at the foot of a form that has said for about a hundred and forty years that nobody is to be sent to find out |
| 2 | 511–520 | 51 incl. | 20,940 | 26.60 | a record kept twice whose copies disagree, and a man whose best number is the wrong number | four names still off a letter, and a 312-word speech a reader will lose |
| 3 | 521–530 | 56 incl. | 20,717 | 26.40 | a man who told a room there was a thing and did not say what the thing was | the word, once, in a box, and the refusal that was changed into a selection in a hand that is not signed |
| 4 | 531–540 | 52 incl. | 17,767 | 22.85 | a man's own wrongdoing as the thing that makes the rest stand up, and a market story he did not make | four true pages with his name at the top, and the second name in the back of a book of carts |
| 5 | 541–550 | 54 incl. | 25,053 | 26.78 | a page with a man's name in a fourth line that is not a fourth head, and a floor nobody washed | a sheet of foolscap with four ruled lines on it, worth more than the whole of what anybody wanted, and a man with a barrow's answer nobody thanked him for |
| **Volume** | **50** | **270 incl.** | **105,396** | **26.11** | **nobody was thanked, and the volume does not notice** | **one finding, in a room's book, under a head that is not its own** |

**The twenty-three chapters the close repaired, and what was wrong in each.** 0501 a narrator saying *Chapter 505* and an interval that was fifteen days and not nine. 0503 *on the page*. 0506 two speech certifications, at −11 and −1. 0509 *eleven words* for a sentence of seventeen. 0510 *one hundred and seventy words in six turns* for five turns of a hundred and thirty-eight. 0511 *four hundred returns* without the hedge, in a figure of a person. 0514 *about four words* for a page of 106. 0521 a claim that nobody had said the four words out loud **at all**, and an interval of *four days before, on a Friday* on a Wednesday. 0523 *on the page*, in the one file in this repository where the word that names the founding paper is written. 0525 *on the page*. 0527 *about about*. 0528 *the first time* where the page prints the second. 0530 *the third time* where the speaker is counting himself. 0540 *for eleven days*. 0542 three sites, of which one is **the word *narration***. 0543 *on the page*. 0544 *on the page*. 0545 two sites, where a page and a bare leaf are confused in the one chapter about what a page costs. 0546 six sites — four gender, one county, one series. 0547 six sites — one trade, five register, of which two are the sentence the Batch 0005 review wrote. 0548 two gender sites. 0549 two sites — *on the page*, and about four hundred and nine standing for people in a market town. 0550 two sites — *on this page*, and a dateline saying the finding is on a page with no head over it in the volume whose resolution is that it is under a head that is not its own.

### What a Volume 12 writer inherits, in one paragraph

**A room that found one thing and cannot do anything and says so out loud. A form with an empty fourth head going out about nine hundred times a month with the line on it. A sheet of foolscap with four ruled lines on it that about four hundred people carried and that has a man's name in the fourth line. A half leaf face down. A hearing with no bench in it. A fifth line nobody ordered. Four documents in four counties and none of them the form. A woman of about twenty-six who has said once that the fourth head ought not to be there and has been right and has not said it again. A woman of about fifty-three who said one thing and was not thanked. A woman of about thirty-one with a pen who carries the first page of a record down nine counties and says she is not going to be thanked for it. A man of about fifty-three with a barrow who answered the room's question and was not thanked. A man of about thirty-four with a sheet who asks a clerk at about a fortieth time and is not thanked. A floor that has not been washed since the eighth of Wolfmonth. Three words for one lane. A shut road four hundred miles off. A city on a shelf. An antagonist nobody named and a plan nobody claimed. Five nines. A twenty-second notch. A register at seven questions and no findings. And a protagonist who is a person a market town can find, and who has said no when he was asked whether he was going to correct the two things about four hundred people each say of him, and who is not thanked, before he leaves or after.**

---

## Volume 12, Batch 0001 — Chapters 551 to 560, "People Who Want That Life"

**Days 1651 to 1679, the twenty-sixth of Mudmonth YR 320 to the twenty-fourth of Greenmonth YR 320, a Sunday to a Sunday, twenty-nine days across ten datelines at days 1651, 1654, 1657, 1662, 1663, 1666, 1669, 1671, 1674 and 1679, four market mornings, two Sundays. Every date in the block is YR 320 and the year does not turn inside Volume 12: Thawmonth the first of YR 321 is day 1956. The block is 18,829 words in ten chapters on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own and the ten added, recounted after the review's repairs. **The block was planned at thirty-six days to day 1686 and runs twenty-nine; days 1680 to 1686 are the first seven of Batch 0002 and `outline/volume-12.md` §8, §17 and the Batch 0002 prompt have all been corrected to say so, and a Batch 0002 writer must not read 1686 as this block's last day.** No crossing is conducted, booked or scheduled in this block, and the volume's outline at `outline/volume-12.md` §16 item 7 is the authority for that.**

**Ch 551 · the twenty-sixth of Mudmonth, YR 320, a Sunday and not a market day · a street in a market town of about two thousand people · nine people from off a made road, a shut room, and one question with a place in it.** **The room over a saddler's is shut on Sundays and the floor has not been washed since the eighth of Wolfmonth.** The man of about fifty-eight with a chain of office that is a badge comes down the stair and says he is not going to open it, and says the rule has held on every Sunday since he came except one, and that he was not in the county on that one. **Aren Kest is asked, for the first time in nine counties, a question about a place — is it safe to send people there — and says *I do not know* and does not add to it, and does not add to it because about four hundred people have carried a sheet with his name in the fourth of its four lines.** A man of about sixty with the nine says out loud that the word in the report is quiet and the word in this town is market and those are not the same word. The woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key says the one thing anybody says, and it is about a town with one rule and no second thing.

**Ch 552 · the twenty-ninth of Mudmonth, YR 320, a Wednesday and not a market day · the room over the saddler's, open · a letter four hands long, eleven people, and a request for a name.** **Merritt Gallow, about fifty-three, arrives with a satchel he does not put down for two days, and the letter asks a man of thirty-two with a limp to put his name at the top of a statement about a road, and asks for a date in the last line, which is not a date.** He says out loud that a certificate is not a fact about a road and is a fact about a man, and that he came a long way to put one on a piece of paper, and that he is not going to be able to say it again in about four years. **Aren Kest asks four questions and none of them is about a form; the fourth is *if a thing is to be certified, who certifies it*, and the answer is the Standing, and the man of about fifty-eight says out loud that the only head in nine counties that takes a name for a road is the fourth one, which has been empty for about five months, and that he is not going to rule a head for it.** A figure of eleven is wanted, a head is ruled over it, and nobody fills it.

**Ch 553 · the second of Greenmonth, YR 320, a Saturday and a market day · the market in the street · eleven strangers put to work, and the first wrong thing anybody hears about that valley.** The fruit of that valley keeps where fruit in nine counties does not, and nobody there throws any away, and the woman of about thirty-one says it the way a person says a thing they are proud of. **The man of about fifty-eight says out loud that a place where nothing is ever thrown away is a place where nothing has ever been decided.** The man of about twenty-nine with a handcart asks whether the road is made and is told no, and is told about four miles of stone channel that has not been mended since before the woman of about thirty-one was born, **and why: because the record of a thing done there about twenty-two years ago is not allowed to be read, and if the record cannot be read then nobody can say the channel wants mending.** The man of about fifty-three writes it on a leaf with no head over it. A woman of about forty-five who does not lift anything all morning asks how many people are in the town and is told to count them off a wall.

**Ch 554 · the seventh of Greenmonth, YR 320, a Thursday and a market day · the room over a saddler's · a leaf about nine inches by six with a mark at the top of it and nothing else on it.** **The mark is not a drawing, a seal or a stamp: it is a scar in a tree, where a branch of one kind was fitted into a cut in another and the wood grew round it for about nine years.** The woman of about thirty-one with a pen says it is the first piece of paper anybody has ever given her that she cannot swear to. **All five documents come onto one table in one breath: the Crown's form with its empty fourth head, the foolscap sheet, the returned form, the piece of stone, and this, and none of the five is the Crown's form, and the fifth line nobody ordered is a fifth line.** The man of about fifty-eight refuses to enter it in a book, because in about four years a clerk would find a line saying a place exists and would have to decide on his own what kind of place. The woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key asks who made the mark, is told it is made every year by whoever holds the knife, and says that a thing made every year is not a mark, it is a name, and that she will not be near it.

**Ch 555 · the eighth of Greenmonth, YR 320, a Friday and not a market day · Merefen, a town of about four thousand people · a sill, a boy with a board, and a bell that has not sounded since the year he was born.** **The recovery chapter, and the block's only day in Aren Kest's own county.** He goes under the third bay from the east at the Old Toll, counts four load points going in and four coming out, tells the man who keeps the yard that the sill wants mending and that the cart has wanted it mended for about eleven years and that both are true at once. He stands under the cracked bell with his left hand flat on the lip of it and says out loud, to nobody, that **a crack does not make a bell unsafe, a crack makes a bell unusable, and a thing nobody can ring is a thing nobody has to answer for**, and that the reason it is safe is that it is broken. **A boy of about seventeen with a rod keeps a column on a board that is not debts, and asks whether water goes where you put it, and the man says he does not know and asks the boy to leave the line on the board and not rub it out and not to be paid for it.**

**Ch 556 · the eleventh of Greenmonth, YR 320, a Monday and not a market day · the room over a saddler's, and the lane outside it · a pot of loam and a scion of about nine inches.** **A woman of about thirty-eight walks up a made road in her hands with a foot of earth out of the lower slope of a valley, and nobody sent her and she is on no sheet in nine counties.** The letter says the oldest tree in the Long Set has been dead for twenty-two years; she cut the scion off it in the second week of the set sitting; a man who has stood in front of that tree has seen nothing in the bark; **the man of about thirty-four with a sheet says both of those are true and refuses to decide which is a lie.** She corrects the letter: the Long Set has eleven terraces and not nine, and the nine is how far a man gets in a day who has walked the slope once and wrote down what he saw. **Asked who told her to send it, she says nobody, and says the Standing has not been told and that she does not know what will happen at the end of the week.**

**Ch 557 · the fourteenth of Greenmonth, YR 320, a Thursday and a market day · the market in the street · the sentence the block is built out of.** **Her name is Ilsa Wend and she keeps a block of ground in the Long Set, which is a portion of a terrace between two head stones, and she has a piece of bark tied to her belt that she has not explained and has not been asked about.** She says: *I would like to change my mind about something, and I do not know what I would change it to.* She grafted four years ago without asking and it took, the Standing told her she had done it wrongly, and **when she opens her mouth to say what she thinks the sentence comes out in the oldest voice in that valley and she can hear it come out and it is not hers; she has had it since she was about nine.** Asked what the Standing would say if she asked to be the person who answers for a road, she says they would be pleased, and that being pleased is not the same as being allowed, and that the two are kept in different buildings. **Asked how she knows, she says nobody told her, and asks for it to be written down that the working-out is hers and is on nobody's sheet.** The copyist of about twenty-six is in the same market copying three filled heads of a return for nothing and refusing to rule the fourth.

**Ch 558 · the sixteenth of Greenmonth, YR 320, a Saturday and a market day · the market in the street · one word said once, and the block's finding.** **A woman of about forty-five from a river city says *continuation* to a man of about twenty-nine with a handcart and will not say it twice, and about four people in that market have said, since the word, that they heard it and about four have said they did not.** The man of about fifty-eight hears it from about nine paces, does not ask what it means, and says out loud that a room which asks a stranger what a word means has stopped keeping a book of answers and started keeping a book of words. **Gallow says the certification cannot be given, says he was sent to find out whether that valley has an answer, and says that the woman nine paces from the door may be it and may not be and that he is not going to spend her in a street.** Gault Prentice says the one thing he has said in four weeks: what has happened to the small room is not that it got bigger, it is that a woman said a word in a street and about nine people in his room have spent the morning on the word instead of the letter. **The review revoiced that speech, which had been lifted whole from the speech that closes Volume 11 at `chapter-0550.md`, and every fact and every figure in it is unchanged.**

**Ch 559 · the nineteenth of Greenmonth, YR 320, a Tuesday and not a market day · the room over a saddler's, and a lane behind it · the pot that opens nothing, and a refusal given in a lane.** At the fourth hour, the hour his palm does the thing he has never told anybody about, **the scion does not move and nothing opens, and the reason is on the page and is not a mechanism: a thing a place has sent and a person has carried for nine days is a thing that has arrived, and a road is not opened by a thing arriving.** The woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key says a question is the only thing in this county that has ever come back with a person in it that nobody in an office chose, and that he has a pot with a tree in it and has not asked one question of a woman who carried a piece of her own place in her hand for nine days. **A leaf is made: four ruled heads, three filled with a pen, the fourth ruled and empty, and the nine words written under it by a person.** At the sixth hour, in a lane, he tells her the certification will not be given and why. **She says: you have told nine counties that my place cannot speak, and you told me in a lane to keep it out of that room, and both of those are true, and you did not ask me whether you would, and you have had nine days. He does not say that he is sorry.** She asks for a sheet, and to write it herself, and says carrying it is the one thing she knows how to do and the one thing nobody has asked her to do.
